# GL₂ modularity lifting: arithmetic patching and its lifting theorems

This roadmap plans the passage from a modular residual representation to an automorphic lift. The main construction couples a universal deformation ring to a Hecke action, introduces Taylor–Wiles levels with integral module control, and patches rings and modules with all their structure maps. Support and local component geometry then turn a deformation point into a Hecke eigensystem. Odd-prime, dyadic, BCDT type-sensitive and modern de Rham lifting routes keep their different hypotheses.

The plan contains 94 local targets: 12 definitions, 14 constructions, 29 lemmas, 32 theorems, six comparisons and one application contract. Its 26 definitions and constructions have 80 API items and 79 discriminating test specifications. Twenty-four targets are selected as planets. These are planning records; every target has implementation status **unchecked**. Ten targets of the revision duplicated lower-tier owners and are retired in their favour (the packet lists them under `retiredDraftNodes`); two more stay only as comparisons for their external consumers. Eleven supplier gaps remain, expressed by 22 open requests and three provided requests. “Complete” means that the assigned scope has been planned; no stage is declared closed.

The packet is [GL2ModularityLifting--R22.1.json](../packets/GL2ModularityLifting--R22.1.json). The [suggested file](../suggested/GL2ModularityLifting--R22.1.lean) gives only light definitions and milestones statable at the pins. The README is the full mathematical specification. The separately accepted [modern part](GL2ModularityLifting--R32.3.md) supplies R32.3–R32.5; its targets are imported below, never duplicated.

## Notation and hypotheses carried between targets

Fix a prime p, a sufficiently large finite extension E/ℚ\_p, its integers O, uniformizer π\_O and finite residue field k. Coefficient enlargement is allowed only with the lattice, residual semisimplification and action comparisons supplied by ArithmeticGaloisRepresentations R01.1. The residual representation is denoted ρ̄, the characteristic-zero representation ρ, and the p-adic cyclotomic character ε or χ\_p. The convention is HT(ε)=1. Oddness means det ρ(c)=−1 at every complex conjugation. At p=2 it must be imposed on ρ itself.

S is a finite set containing the ramified places, the p-adic places and the relevant infinite places in the global deformation problem. A bar on a deformation ring denotes the prescribed local-condition quotient, never a reduced quotient unless stated. A square denotes the chosen framing. T denotes the faithful image of the Hecke action on the specified localized coefficient module. A “modular residual representation” comes with an actual cuspidal eigensystem witness. Conditions α and β are different witness predicates, specified below. No theorem proving Serre’s conjecture is an input to the independent lifting route.

At Taylor–Wiles level Q, Δ′\_Q is the product of maximal p-quotients of residue-unit groups. If N bounds the p-primary stabilizer exponents, Δ\_Q=Δ′\_Q/Δ′\_Q[N]. The ring may carry Δ′\_Q while the free module carries Δ\_Q. Scalar variables y_1,…,y_h correspond to the latter diamonds; the last j variables are framing variables. Write S∞=O[[y_1,…,y_{h+j}]] and J=(y_1,…,y_h). A power ideal m_C^(r) is generated by r-th powers of elements of m_C. It is different from the ordinary ideal power m_C^r.

All statements use the hypotheses of the explicitly listed input data. A target’s prerequisite list imports a supplier contract, not a completed Lean implementation. A stage-level reference with an open request is conditional on that exact request. The target sections give their extra hypotheses, proof route, source locators, APIs, Checks and tests.

## Library baseline and ownership

The audited pins are Mathlib `082e2d37e8b0463410cdb532e111cd43d5a66174` and Tau Ceti `f790474821cf4256814db967cb154e7af3d0c369`. Full declarations, rather than names alone, were checked at these pins. Current TauCetiRoadmap main `0d144d044dacebca17b654695b6f1ad58f159b27` and Tau Ceti main `a91d3aafa8cd3e6bc33dfde0d7677ed0f1625039` were also inspected read-only, including the nine newer roadmaps absent from the atlas snapshot. Their existing mathematics is imported.

- `mathlib:Module.Free` in `Mathlib/LinearAlgebra/FreeModule/Basic.lean`: Free modules.
- `mathlib:MonoidAlgebra` in `Mathlib/Algebra/MonoidAlgebra/Defs.lean`: Group algebras 𝒪[Δ].
- `mathlib:Group.IsSolvable` in `Mathlib/GroupTheory/Solvable.lean`: Solvable groups.
- `mathlib:Group.isSolvable_of_ker_le_range` in `Mathlib/GroupTheory/Solvable.lean`: For homomorphisms f : G′ → G and g : G → G″ with ker g contained in range f, solvability of G′ and G″ implies solvability of G. The Borel-solvability bridge is separate.
- `mathlib:Group.isSolvable_of_isSolvable_injective` in `Mathlib/GroupTheory/Solvable.lean`: A subgroup (injective image) of a solvable group is solvable.
- `mathlib:ZMod.neg_one_ne_one` in `Mathlib/Data/ZMod/Basic.lean`: −1 ≠ 1 in ZMod n for n > 2.
- `tauceti:TauCeti.UpperTriangularGroup.instIsSolvable` in `TauCeti/LinearAlgebra/Matrix/GeneralLinearGroup/UpperTriangular/Solvable.lean`: For a finite linearly ordered index type m and commutative ring R, the upper-triangular subgroup of GL_m(R) is solvable; specialize to m=Fin 2.
- `tauceti:TauCeti.GlobalNumberFields.weakApproximation_denseRange` in `TauCeti/NumberTheory/NumberField/Global/Approximation/Weak.lean`: For a number field K and finite sets of finite and infinite places, the diagonal map from K to the product of their completions has dense range. Applying this to the open square classes and positive real rays prescribes quadratic completions and total reality.

Tau Ceti’s upper-triangular solvability and weak approximation are already present; they are not new targets here. Finite coefficient descent and stable lattices are already planned by ArithmeticGaloisRepresentations R01.1. The generic Taylor–Wiles levels, stabilizer control, selected-root comparisons, the Hensel root with localized Δ\_Q-freeness and coinvariant control (R18.3/tw-localised-control, KW II Corollary 7.5) and norm twists are owned by HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves R18.3. This roadmap owns their application at the actual Taylor–Wiles primes and their compatibility with the deformation actions.

The commutative-algebra arguments import the current accepted DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra R03.1/R03.3–R03.6 contracts: finite-module completion, finite-map depth comparison, completed tensor components, structured inverse limits, support, multiplicities and faithful quotient identification, together with Kisin’s patching datum, ring-module patching, characteristic-zero criterion and regular-base criterion of R03.5 (Kisin Proposition (3.3.1)), of which the R22.3 targets are the arithmetic instances. The classical CDT numerical module criterion is a distinct requested extension of R03.5. Local Galois deformation geometry belongs to R08, coefficient forms to R18, local–global compatibility to R19, weight/type changes to R20, ordinary lifting to R21, and the modern local/global module inputs to R30/R31. The requests below state precisely what is still needed from each.

KW II §8 — the initial field of Lemma 8.1, the determinant kinds of §8.1, Theorems 8.2 and 8.4, Lemma 8.3 and the quaternionic level raising — and allowable base change (Definition 7.9) are planned by the tier-19 SerreWeightAndLevelOptimisation R20.6 (§§6.6–6.11 of TauCetiRoadmap pull request #1013, whose README names this roadmap as consumer) and are imported here. KW II Lemma 7.10 is ArithmeticGaloisDuality R02.6, and CHT Lemma 4.1.1 is a case of its Grunwald–Wang theorem R02.4. This tier-21 roadmap owns CHT Lemma 4.1.2 and the existence of allowable changes with prescribed completions; PotentialModularityAndCompatibleSystems R23.1 imports them downward. No prerequisite points to a higher roadmap.

The support theorem in R22.3 supplies a union of top-dimensional components. Full support is proved only under its domain hypotheses, or through the separate avoidance/dyadic component arguments. Generic-fibre R=T does not automatically give integral R=T. The integral refinement needs the global presentation bound and a regular sequence. The dyadic argument first patches the ambient determinant-varying space; only its 2-torsion torus action preserves the determinant-one fibre.

The BCDT route uses corrected type-coefficient cohomology and the classical numerical module criterion. It retains admission, weak acceptability and scalar local centralizer hypotheses. The modern multiplicity argument retains the corrected generic rank 2^{|R|}, weight-specific support and the normalized lower bound with its local exclusion. The exceptional p=3 route is separate and remains conditional on the listed modern suppliers.

## Worked examples and failure tests

1. **Removing isotropy.** For Δ′=ℤ/27ℤ and N=3, the N-torsion subgroup is {0,9,18}; Δ′/Δ′[3] has order 9. Quotienting by third powers instead would give order 3. A definition making the latter quotient fails the intended diamond-group test.
2. **Choosing an actual root.** Over ℤ₅ the polynomial X²−1 has simple residual root 1. Its Hensel lift on that branch is 1. The coefficient representative 6 has the same reduction but evaluates to 35, which is nonzero in ℤ₅. It can specify the branch ideal, but it is not the selected Hecke root.
3. **Patching bounds.** With s=1, p=3, m=2 and h+j=1, the length bound is r_m=18. In 𝔽₂[[x,y]], the ideal generated by squares of maximal-ideal elements is (x²,y²), while the ordinary square also contains xy. Keeping these ideals distinct is necessary when constructing a common finite quotient A/m_A^{r′\_m}.
4. **Component support.** For A=k[[x,y]]/(xy), the module A/(x) has depth and dimension one, but misses the component defined by y. Maximal Cohen–Macaulay depth alone cannot prove full support on a reducible ring. The domain argument, avoidance transfer or twist-transitivity must supply the missing conclusion.
5. **Framing and augmentation.** For |S|=1, j=3. A finite-presentation module M becomes M[[y₁,y₂,y₃]], and quotient by these variables recovers M. Sending the variables to zero in the framed map itself would give augmentation, not the specified base-change map.
6. **Dyadic oddness.** The characteristic-zero involution diag(1,−1) is odd and reduces to the identity modulo 2. Conversely the identity matrix has residual determinant −1 in characteristic 2 but has characteristic-zero determinant +1. Residual determinant sign cannot test oddness at 2.
7. **Extended types.** When ℓ is odd and w²=1, the projector (1+w)/2 selects the required +1 coefficient summand. At ℓ=2 its denominator is not invertible; the same construction is not an integral splitting. The BCDT route assumes ℓ>2.
8. **Corrected multiplicities.** If two auxiliary places have equal residual Frobenius eigenvalues, |R|=2 and the generic rank is four. The comparison uses one quarter of the module multiplicity. It does not replace the normalized lower bound for each graded factor by an unconditional equality.
9. **Weight normalization.** Weights {3,7} become {0,4} after twisting by ε^(−3), so the normalized modular weight is five. In the signed-prime convention 3\*=−3 and 5\*=5; the expression at 2 is defined arithmetically, but the quadratic cyclotomic theorem assumes an odd prime.

Tests are specifications with explicit expected outcomes, not a claim that arithmetic tests already elaborate. The few active pin-level examples are described after the target catalogue.

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.5-kw-residual-modularity"></a>

### Residual modularity of minimal weight and unramified level

**Definition.** `R22.5/kw-residual-modularity`.

For F totally real and the residual representation ρ̄ of KW lifting data, ResidualModularAlpha(ρ̄) means there exists a cuspidal π of GL₂(𝔸\_F) with ρ̄\_π≅ρ̄, parallel weight k(ρ̄), and π\_v unramified at every v|p. This is a predicate on witnesses, not an assertion that every modular witness has minimal weight.

**Hypotheses.**

- F is totally real and ρ̄ : G_F → GL_2(𝔽) is as in KW II §8, with lifting data and determinant character ψ (GlobalGaloisDeformations R04.6/kw-deformation-data; the local conditions are LocalGaloisDeformationRings R08.6/kw-local-conditions).

**Ownership.** SerreWeightAndLevelOptimisation R20.6/kw-optimisation-data records the α and β witnesses as fields of the KW II §8 data; this node is the predicate that the GL2 lifting theorems take as hypothesis and that PotentialModularityAndCompatibleSystems R23.4 and R24.4 cite. A witness of this predicate is exactly what fills the corresponding field. The stable R22.5 identifier is retained with parent R22.1.

**Prerequisites.** `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.6/kw-deformation-data`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2`.

**Proof route.**

- Define the existential predicate using the Hilbert automorphic representation and its attached Galois representation from R19.2. It precedes construction of minimal-level-data; no residual-modularity theorem or optimized witness is part of the definition.

**API and Checks.** Instantiate the coefficient ring, representation, fields, levels and actions specified above. Check the following signatures over those actual objects; no existential witness or conclusion may be added as an input merely to satisfy a Check.

- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.ResidualModularAlpha` (constructor): (α): ρ̄ ≅ ρ̄\_π with π unramified above p, of weight k(ρ̄).
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.residualModularAlpha_iff` (characterisation): The predicate holds iff a cuspidal witness of weight k(ρ̄), unramified at all v|p, with residual representation ρ̄ exists.
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.residualModularAlpha_of_witness` (constructor): A witness with the stated residual representation, weight and level gives ResidualModularAlpha(ρ̄).

**Unit-test specifications.**

- `alpha_of_goodReduction` (compatibility): A modular elliptic curve with good reduction at p and k(ρ̄)=2 supplies an α-witness.
- `alpha_weight_must_match` (non-example): An unramified weight k(ρ̄)+p−1 eigenform is not itself an α-witness, since its weight differs from k(ρ̄).
- `steinberg_not_alpha` (non-example): A Steinberg eigenform at p is not an α-witness, while its conductor-p component is allowed in a β-witness. This concerns that witness, not nonexistence of every α-witness for ρ̄.

**Acceptance checks.**

- ρ̄ = ρ̄\_E for an elliptic curve E/ℚ with good reduction at p: E's newform is unramified at p of weight 2, so (α) holds when k(ρ̄) = 2 and (β) holds.
- At weight two an α-witness also witnesses β. A Steinberg witness has conductor p, so that particular witness satisfies β's local condition but not α's; another unramified witness for the same residual representation is not ruled out.
- A residual witness of weight k(ρ̄) + p − 1, unramified at p, witnesses neither (α) nor (β): the weight at infinity must be k(ρ̄), resp. 2.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), §8.2, p. 70 — §8.2, p. 70: hypothesis (α), that ρ̄|G_F arises from a cuspidal π unramified at all v | p and discrete series of weight k(ρ̄) at infinity, followed by (β), weight two with conductor dividing v above p..

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.5-kw-residual-modularity-beta"></a>

### Residual modularity of weight two and conductor at most one

**Definition.** `R22.5/kw-residual-modularity-beta`.

For F totally real and the residual representation ρ̄ of KW lifting data, ResidualModularBeta(ρ̄) means there exists a cuspidal π of GL₂(𝔸\_F) with ρ̄\_π≅ρ̄, parallel weight two, and conductor exponent at most one at every v|p.

**Hypotheses.**

- F is totally real and ρ̄ : G_F → GL_2(𝔽) is as in KW II §8, with lifting data and determinant character ψ (GlobalGaloisDeformations R04.6/kw-deformation-data; the local conditions are LocalGaloisDeformationRings R08.6/kw-local-conditions).

**Ownership.** SerreWeightAndLevelOptimisation R20.6/kw-optimisation-data records the α and β witnesses as fields of the KW II §8 data; this node is the predicate that the GL2 lifting theorems take as hypothesis and that PotentialModularityAndCompatibleSystems R23.4 and R24.4 cite. A witness of this predicate is exactly what fills the corresponding field. The stable R22.5 identifier is retained with parent R22.1.

**Prerequisites.** `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.6/kw-deformation-data`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2`.

**Proof route.**

- Define the existential predicate using the Hilbert automorphic representation and its attached Galois representation from R19.2. It precedes construction of minimal-level-data; no residual-modularity theorem or optimized witness is part of the definition.

**API and Checks.** Instantiate the coefficient ring, representation, fields, levels and actions specified above. Check the following signatures over those actual objects; no existential witness or conclusion may be added as an input merely to satisfy a Check.

- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.ResidualModularBeta` (constructor): The existential predicate with the stated weight-two and conductor conditions.
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.residualModularBeta_iff` (characterisation): ResidualModularBeta(ρ̄) iff a cuspidal π with residual representation ρ̄, weight two and local conductor exponent ≤1 exists.
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.residualModularBeta_of_witness` (constructor): A π satisfying those three conditions gives ResidualModularBeta(ρ̄).

**Unit-test specifications.**

- `beta_of_unramified_weight_two` (compatibility): A weight-two unramified local witness has conductor exponent zero and hence satisfies the β local bound.
- `beta_allows_steinberg_witness` (characterisation): A weight-two Steinberg component of conductor exponent one is allowed in a β-witness.
- `conductor_two_not_beta_witness` (non-example): A weight-two eigenform with conductor exponent two at a place above p is not itself a β-witness; this does not preclude another witness.

**Acceptance checks.**

- ρ̄ = ρ̄\_E for an elliptic curve E/ℚ with good reduction at p: E's newform is unramified at p of weight 2, so (α) holds when k(ρ̄) = 2 and (β) holds.
- At weight two an α-witness also witnesses β. A Steinberg witness has conductor p, so that particular witness satisfies β's local condition but not α's; another unramified witness for the same residual representation is not ruled out.
- A residual witness of weight k(ρ̄) + p − 1, unramified at p, witnesses neither (α) nor (β): the weight at infinity must be k(ρ̄), resp. 2.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), §8.2, p. 70 — Hypothesis (β), p.70: the cuspidal residual witness has conductor exponent at most one above p and parallel weight two. This is not the preceding hypothesis (α)..


<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-alpha-beta-under-allowable-base-change"></a>

### The residual hypotheses (α) and (β) persist under allowable base change

**Lemma.** `R22.1/alpha-beta-under-allowable-base-change`.

Let F and ρ̄ be as in KW II §7.6.2 and F′/F an allowable base change. If ρ̄|\_{G_F} satisfies (α) with witness π, then ρ̄|\_{G_{F′}} satisfies (α) with witness the base change π\_{F′}; likewise for (β). More precisely π\_{F′} is cuspidal, it is discrete series of the same parallel weight at every infinite place of F′, ρ̄\_{π\_{F′}} ≅ ρ̄\_π|\_{G_{F′}}, and for places w | v | p: π\_{F′,w} is unramified if π\_v is, and its conductor exponent is at most 1 if that of π\_v is.

**Hypotheses.**

- Allowable changes are those of SerreWeightAndLevelOptimisation R20.6/kw-optimisation-data (KW II Definition 7.9). KW II do not define "solvable"; what their constructions give, and what is used here, is a tower F = F₀ ⊂ … ⊂ F_n = F′ of Galois steps with soluble groups, so that base change can be applied along cyclic steps of prime degree. Image preservation is the relative condition ρ̄(G_{F′}) = ρ̄(G_F) with ρ̄|G_{F′(μ\_p)} absolutely irreducible.
- Cuspidality of each base change uses that the restriction of ρ̄ stays absolutely irreducible (the image condition of an allowable change): a cuspidal π whose base change along a cyclic extension is not cuspidal is automorphically induced from that extension, and then ρ\_π restricted to it is reducible.
- F′/F is unramified above p, and for an unramified local extension the conductor exponent of the local base change equals that of π\_v.
- The definitions of (α) and (β) do not contain this transport; KW II use it without comment whenever they 'reinitialise' the base field.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.5/kw-residual-modularity](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.5-kw-residual-modularity), [R22.5/kw-residual-modularity-beta](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.5-kw-residual-modularity-beta), `SerreWeightAndLevelOptimisation:R20.6/kw-optimisation-data`, `GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer:R17.4`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.2`.

**Proof route.**

- Refine the soluble tower of the allowable change to cyclic steps of prime degree, each Galois over the previous field and totally real.
- For one cyclic step apply Langlands' base change (GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer R17.4): it exists, is compatible with local base change at every place, and is cuspidal by the irreducibility remark above.
- Local behaviour. At infinite places the extension of completions is ℝ/ℝ and the weight is unchanged. At w | v | p the extension F′\_w/F_v is unramified: an unramified principal series stays unramified, and the conductor exponent is unchanged in general.
- Galois side. ρ\_{π\_{F′}} ≅ ρ\_π|\_{G_{F′}} by comparison of Frobenius traces at the unramified places (AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations R19.2), hence the same for the residual representations.

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- F′/F quadratic, π unramified at v | p inert in F′: π\_{F′,w} is unramified, so an (α)-witness base-changes to an (α)-witness.
- A Steinberg component at v | p (conductor exponent 1) base-changes along an unramified extension to a Steinberg component: a (β)-witness stays a (β)-witness and does not become an (α)-witness.
- If the residual image shrank over F′ so that ρ̄|\_{G_{F′}} were reducible, π\_{F′} could fail to be cuspidal; this is excluded by the definition of allowable.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), §7.6.2, after Definition 7.9, p. 68 — Why allowable base changes are harmless: base change transports the automorphic witnesses..



<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-cht-soluble-prescribed-completions"></a>

### Soluble extensions with prescribed completions

**Theorem.** `R22.1/cht-soluble-prescribed-completions`.

Let F be a number field, D/F finite Galois, S a finite set of places, and E_v/F_v finite Galois for each v in S (at real places either R or C). There is a finite soluble Galois E/F linearly disjoint from D/F such that for every w above v in S, E_w is isomorphic to E_v as an F_v-algebra. Taking E_v=R at every real place makes E totally real when F is totally real. No assertion of cyclic E of a prescribed degree is made.

**Hypotheses.**

- At a real place v, E_v is ℝ or ℂ; at a finite place E_v/F_v is any finite Galois extension, hence soluble. D/F is finite Galois.

**Prerequisites.** `ArithmeticGaloisDuality:R02.4/grunwald-wang`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/Chebotarev#layer-10-dirichlet-density-chebotarev`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/ClassFieldTheory#layer-12-separate-arithmetic-global-existence-the-norm-index-and-the-global-correspondence`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/ClassFieldTheory#layer-7-the-absolute-local-artin-map-its-normalizations-and-conductors`.

**Proof route.**

- Add split conditions at a nonsplit prime for each simple Galois subextension of D, by Chebotarev.
- Induct on the maximum of the local extension degrees. Finite Galois groups of nonarchimedean local fields are soluble, so choose a nontrivial cyclic quotient at each nonsplit place.
- Realise each cyclic local first step: by local class field theory (Tau Ceti ClassFieldTheory, Layer 7) choose a finite-order character χ\_S of ∏\_{v∈S} F_v^× whose kernel at v corresponds to the cyclic E_v/F_v; extend it to a finite-order idele class character by the Grunwald–Wang theorem (ArithmeticGaloisDuality R02.4/grunwald-wang; the possible doubling of the order is harmless); let the cyclic extension be its class field (Layer 12). Apply induction to the remaining local extensions.
- Take the normal closure of the composite over F; local normality ensures the completions remain E_v, solvability is preserved, and the added split conditions force disjointness.

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For all L_v=K_v check complete splitting, and for a real place prescribed R check that all resulting real completions remain R. The avoidance field must be included at each cyclic tower step.
- If every E_v = F_v, the lemma gives a soluble E/F, linearly disjoint from D, in which every v ∈ S splits completely; E = F is allowed.
- Prescribing E_v = ℝ at every real place of a totally real F makes E totally real.

**Sources.** [CHT-2008](https://www.numdam.org/item/10.1007/s10240-008-0016-1.pdf), Lemma 4.1.2, statement p. 116 and proof p. 117 — Lemma 4.1.2, p. 116: for F a number field, D/F finite Galois and finite Galois E_v/F_v at the places of a finite S, there is a finite soluble Galois E/F linearly disjoint from D with E_w ≅ E_v over F_v for all w | v ∈ S; the proof on p. 117 adds non-split primes for the simple subextensions of D, inducts on the local degrees to reach cyclic E_v, and realises them by extending a local character (Lemma 4.1.1) and global class field theory..

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-allowable-base-change-existence"></a>

### Existence of allowable base changes with prescribed completions

**Lemma.** `R22.1/allowable-base-change-existence`.

Let F and ρ̄ be as in KW II §7.6.2 (F totally real, unramified at p, split at p if ρ̄|\_{D_p} is irreducible; ρ̄|\_{G_F} of non-solvable image if p = 2, and ρ̄|\_{G_{F(μ\_p)}} absolutely irreducible if p > 2). Let S₀ be a finite set of finite places of F containing the places above p, and for v ∈ S₀ let E_v/F_v be a finite Galois extension, unramified if v | p and equal to F_v if v | p and ρ̄|\_{D_p} is irreducible. Let L/F be a finite extension. Then there is a finite Galois extension F′/F with soluble Galois group such that: (1) F′ is totally real; (2) [F′ : F] is even; (3) F′\_w ≅ E_v over F_v for every v ∈ S₀ and every place w | v of F′, so that F′/F is unramified above p, split at each v with E_v = F_v, and split above p when ρ̄|\_{D_p} is irreducible; (4) F′ is linearly disjoint over F from L, from the fixed field K of ker ρ̄|\_{G_F} and from K(μ\_p), so that ρ̄(G_{F′}) = ρ̄(G_F) and ρ̄(G_{F′(μ\_p)}) = ρ̄(G_{F(μ\_p)}). (5) If every E_v, v ∈ S₀, has degree at most 2 over F_v, then F′ can be taken quadratic over F. In particular F′/F is an allowable change in the sense of SerreWeightAndLevelOptimisation R20.6/kw-optimisation-data (KW II Definition 7.9), with the prescribed completions at S₀. The base F′ again satisfies the conditions of §7.6.2, so the statement can be applied repeatedly, and the resulting towers are allowable over F (kwOptimisationData_allowable_comp).

**Hypotheses.**

- At a place above p only an unramified E_v may be prescribed, and only the trivial one when ρ̄|\_{D_p} is irreducible. A nontrivial unramified E_v above p occurs in Lemma 8.1 (to make ρ̄ trivial above p when it is unramified there), in the dyadic branch with k(ρ̄) = 4 and in type (C) with k(ρ̄) = 2, the two cases in which Theorem 8.4 does not promise splitting at p.
- Evenness comes from one auxiliary place with the unramified quadratic extension prescribed, not from the places of S₀; without it F′ = F would satisfy (1), (3) and (4).
- KW II's second criterion, for dihedral projective image (a prime split in the field cut out by the projective image and inert in F(μ\_p)), is not needed: disjointness from K(μ\_p) preserves the image over the cyclotomic field.
- KW II cite Lemma 2.2 of Taylor's 'On icosahedral Artin representations II' for this; that paper was not read. The statement is derived here from Clozel–Harris–Taylor's Lemma 4.1.2 (R22.1/cht-soluble-prescribed-completions), whose cyclic steps use the Grunwald–Wang theorem of ArithmeticGaloisDuality R02.4.
- No automorphic input is used: this is existence of a field, not GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer R17.4's base change.
- 'ρ̄|\_{D_p} is irreducible' means absolutely irreducible, as everywhere in KW II §§7–8: an unramified ρ̄|\_{D_p} has cyclic image and is reducible over 𝔽̄\_p.
- Clause (5) matters: Lemma 4.1.2 controls the completions but not the global degree, and the proof of Theorem 8.4 needs extensions of degree exactly 2 (KW II p. 77: each F_i/F_{i−1} is quadratic, with one place above v_i's neighbours and two above v_i). For exponent 2 no class field theory is needed, only weak approximation.

**Prerequisites.** `SerreWeightAndLevelOptimisation:R20.6/kw-optimisation-data`, [R22.1/cht-soluble-prescribed-completions](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-cht-soluble-prescribed-completions), `ArithmeticGaloisRepresentations:R01.4`, `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/Chebotarev#layer-10-dirichlet-density-chebotarev`, `tauceti:TauCeti.GlobalNumberFields.weakApproximation_denseRange`.

**Proof route.**

- Auxiliary place. Choose a finite place u of F outside S₀, not above p, and let E_u be the unramified quadratic extension of F_u.
- Apply Clozel–Harris–Taylor's Lemma 4.1.2 (R22.1/cht-soluble-prescribed-completions) to F, to D the Galois closure over F of L·K(μ\_p), and to the set S = S₀ ∪ {u} ∪ {real places of F}, with the given E_v at S₀, E_u at u and E_v = F_v = ℝ at the real places. It gives a finite soluble Galois F′/F, linearly disjoint from D, with F′\_w ≅ E_v for all w | v ∈ S.
- Totally real: every real place of F splits completely in F′. Even degree: F′/F is Galois, so the local degree [F′\_w : F_u] = 2 divides [F′ : F]. The conditions at p are read off (3).
- Images. F′ is linearly disjoint from K(μ\_p) over F, so restriction is an isomorphism Gal(F′K(μ\_p)/F′) ≅ Gal(K(μ\_p)/F) carrying the subgroup that fixes μ\_p to the subgroup that fixes μ\_p. Hence ρ̄(G_{F′}) = ρ̄(G_F) and ρ̄(G_{F′(μ\_p)}) = ρ̄(G_{F(μ\_p)}); absolute irreducibility over F′(μ\_p), and non-solvability at p = 2, are properties of these images (ArithmeticGaloisRepresentations R01.4).
- Quadratic case (5). Write E_v = F_v(√d_v) with d_v ∈ F_v^× (d_v = 1 if E_v = F_v). Choose a finite place z of F outside S₀, not above 2, that splits completely in D (Chebotarev for the trivial class; Tau Ceti Chebotarev, Layer 10), and a non-square unit d_z of F_z. The squares are open in each F_v^×, so by weak approximation there is d ∈ F^× that is totally positive, lies in d_v·(F_v^×)² for v ∈ S₀ and in d_z·(F_z^×)². Put F′ = F(√d). It is totally real, quadratic (z is inert), with completions E_v at S₀; and F′ is not contained in D, because z is inert in F′ and splits completely in D, so F′ ∩ D = F. No auxiliary place u is needed, since the degree is 2.
- Iteration. F′ is totally real, unramified at p and split at p when required, with the same residual images, so §7.6.2 holds over F′; a tower of such steps is allowable over F by the composition of allowable changes (SerreWeightAndLevelOptimisation R20.6/kw-optimisation-data, kwOptimisationData_allowable_comp).

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- F = ℚ, p = 5, S₀ = {5} with E₅ = ℚ₅, u = 3: ℚ(√11) is real quadratic, 5 splits in it (11 ≡ 1 mod 5) and 3 is inert (11 ≡ 2 mod 3). Disjointness from K is a further condition, which is why D enters the construction.
- With S₀ = {v | p} and every E_v = F_v the lemma gives the allowable base changes 'split at p' of Theorem 8.2.
- Prescribing the unramified extension of degree m at the places of a finite set makes the p-part of the residue-field unit groups there as large as required; this is the use in the proof of Theorem 8.2 (order divisible by the p-part of 2p(4N_w)).
- Over the field F_{i−1} of the proof of Theorem 8.4, clause (5) with the trivial extension at the places above v_i and the unramified quadratic extension at the places above the other v_j gives a quadratic F_i, with v_j inert for j ≠ i and split for j = i. Without clause (5) the degree of F_i could be a larger even number, and the count of places above v_1, …, v_i in the proof would be wrong.
- Without the real places in S the extension could be totally complex, and the Hilbert modular setting would be lost.

**Sources.** [CHT-2008](https://www.numdam.org/item/10.1007/s10240-008-0016-1.pdf), Lemma 4.1.2, statement p. 116 and proof p. 117 — The existence theorem applied; S may contain real places.; [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), §7.6.2, paragraph after Definition 7.9, p. 68 — KW II's sufficient condition for the image conditions; the node proves it and strengthens it to disjointness from the field cut out with the p-th roots of unity.; [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), §7.6.2, p. 68 — The existence statement is left to the reader in the source (the spelling is the source's)..




<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-theorem-8-2-minimal-modular-lifts"></a>

### Theorem 8.2: the minimal modular lifts imported from SerreWeightAndLevelOptimisation

**Comparison.** `R22.1/theorem-8-2-minimal-modular-lifts`.

KW II Theorem 8.2 is SerreWeightAndLevelOptimisation R20.6/kw-minimal-modular-lifts, stated on the data of R20.6/kw-optimisation-data: for p > 2 both α and β, for p = 2 β and α when k(ρ̄) = 2; Σ a subset of the Steinberg places of the chosen witness (containing its Steinberg places above p in the β branch with k(ρ̄) = 2); after an allowable change F″/F, split at p when p > 2, a cuspidal π″ lifting ρ̄|G_{F″}, spherical outside Σ ∪ {v | p}, Steinberg above Σ, with central character ψ\_{F″} and the p-local cases (a) weight k(ρ̄) with ψ of kind (i), (b) weight two with U₁(v) invariants when k(ρ̄) < p + 1, (c) weight two with U₀(v) invariants when k(ρ̄) = p + 1, and for p = 2 weight two, Steinberg at every place above 2 when k(ρ̄) = 4. This roadmap plans none of it: the identifier is kept because PotentialModularityAndCompatibleSystems R23.3 and R24.1 cite it, and they read it as exactly that statement.

**Hypotheses.**

- The hypotheses are those of the owner node, including the actual α and β witnesses (R22.5/kw-residual-modularity and kw-residual-modularity-beta are the predicates whose witnesses fill the α/β fields of R20.6/kw-optimisation-data).
- At p = 2 and k(ρ̄) = 4 the U_v-eigenvalue is the chosen unramified square root of ψ\_v after restriction, and the final change need not split above 2 (KW II p. 73); the owner keeps both clauses.

**Prerequisites.** `SerreWeightAndLevelOptimisation:R20.6/kw-minimal-modular-lifts`, `SerreWeightAndLevelOptimisation:R20.6/kw-optimisation-data`, [R22.5/kw-residual-modularity](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.5-kw-residual-modularity), [R22.5/kw-residual-modularity-beta](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.5-kw-residual-modularity-beta).

**Proof route.**

- Read the conclusion off SerreWeightAndLevelOptimisation R20.6/kw-minimal-modular-lifts; its proof (stabiliser control, twisted coefficient spaces, Lemma 7.10 through ArithmeticGaloisDuality R02.6, Lemma 8.3 for the extra weight-p+1 branch) is the owner's.
- Consumers that need only the existence of a minimal cuspidal witness over an allowable extension, as PotentialModularityAndCompatibleSystems R23.3 does, cite the owner node directly; this node adds no hypothesis and no conclusion.

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- At k(ρ̄) = p + 1 the owner uses case (c), weight two with U₀(v) invariants, not case (b).
- For a β witness with k(ρ̄) = 2, Σ contains the Steinberg places of the witness above p.
- Nothing in this packet re-proves Theorem 8.2; the GL2 lifting theorems use the owner's Theorem 8.4 (R20.6/prescribed-local-type-lifts) directly.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), Theorem 8.2, statement p. 71, proof pp. 71–74 — States the minimal modular lift after allowable base change in the three odd-p cases and the dyadic case, with the Steinberg set Σ and the central character fixed, and proves all cases by one quaternionic argument; the owner node follows this statement..



<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-minimal-level-data"></a>

### Minimal-level data for a modular residual representation

**Definition.** `R22.1/minimal-level-data`.

Let ρ̄ : G_F → GL_2(𝔽) satisfy KW II's hypotheses with lifting data and ψ (GlobalGaloisDeformations R04.6/kw-deformation-data), and suppose, after an allowable base change, that F is totally real of even degree with [F : ℚ] + |Σ| even. Minimal-level data consist of: the definite quaternion algebra D/F ramified exactly at Σ and the infinite places; the level U = ∏\_v U_v, maximal compact outside S, (𝒪\_D)\_v^× at v ∈ Σ (D_v^× when p = 2), and at v | p maximal compact or U_1(v) according to type (A) or (B); the weight module W_k (k = 2 if p = 2, with W_2 extended to U(𝔸\_F^∞)^× when U is not compact); and a non-Eisenstein maximal ideal 𝔪 ⊂ 𝕋\_ψ(U) such that ρ̄\_𝔪 ≅ ρ̄ and 𝔪 comes from a cuspidal π that fits the lifting data. The data record a residual modularity witness (actual eigenform data), not an R = T statement.

**Hypotheses.**

- The spaces S_{k,ψ}(U, 𝒪), the Hecke algebra 𝕋\_ψ(U) and non-Eisenstein ideals are HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves R18.3, exported by R18.6 (requested).
- The existence of the cuspidal π fitting KW lifting data is SerreWeightAndLevelOptimisation R20.6/prescribed-local-type-lifts (KW II Theorem 8.4), conditional on its α/β witnesses and admissible local data.
- The roadmap convention: 'a residual modularity witness is actual eigenform/Hecke data.'

**Prerequisites.** `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.6/kw-deformation-data`, `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:R18.6`, `SerreWeightAndLevelOptimisation:R20.6/prescribed-local-type-lifts`, `GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer:R17.3/global-jl`.

**Proof route.**

- A definite D ramified exactly at Σ ∪ {∞} exists because [F : ℚ] + |Σ| is even (Hasse's parity condition).
- π transfers to D^× by Jacquet–Langlands (π is discrete series at Σ), and its Hecke eigenvalues cut out 𝔪.

**API and Checks.** Instantiate the coefficient ring, representation, fields, levels and actions specified above. Check the following signatures over those actual objects; no existential witness or conclusion may be added as an input merely to satisfy a Check.

- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.MinimalLevelData` (structure): (D, U, W_k, ψ, 𝔪) with ρ̄\_𝔪 ≅ ρ̄ and 𝔪 from π fitting the lifting data.
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.MinimalLevelData.heckeAlgebra` (constructor): 𝕋 = 𝕋\_ψ(U)\_𝔪.
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.MinimalLevelData.heckeModule` (constructor): M = S_{k,ψ}(U, 𝒪)\_𝔪.
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.MinimalLevelData.residual_iso` (characterisation): ρ̄\_𝔪 ≅ ρ̄.

**Unit-test specifications.**

- `minimalLevel_p2_noncompact` (characterisation): p = 2: U_v = D_v^× at v ∈ Σ and U is not compact.
- `minimalLevel_residual` (characterisation): The residual representation attached to 𝔪 is ρ̄.
- `minimalLevel_no_RT_field` (non-example): No field of the structure states R̄^ψ\_S ≅ 𝕋\_ψ(U)\_𝔪.

**Acceptance checks.**

- p = 2: U_v = D_v^× at v ∈ Σ, so U is not compact, and W_2 is extended in one of 2^{|Σ|} ways (KW II §7).
- Gee's setting (p ≥ 5): Σ = ∅ after base change, and U_v = GL_2(𝒪\_{F_v}) outside T.
- A datum whose fields assert R ≅ 𝕋 is not minimal-level data: only π and 𝔪 are recorded.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), §9.1.1, pp. 78–79 — §9.1.1, pp. 78–79: Σ, S, the definite quaternion algebra ramified exactly at Σ and the infinite places, the level U (maximal compact outside S, (O_D)\_v^× at Σ, or D_v^× when p = 2, and maximal compact or U_1(v) above p by type), the extension of W_2 when p = 2, and the maximal ideal 𝔪 of 𝕋\_ψ(U) given by π.; [GEE-MLT-2022](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2202.05818v2), §5.6, p. 35 — §5.6, p. 35: Gee's levels U_Q (GL₂(O_{F_v}) outside Q ∪ T_r, U_0(v) at T_r, and the Δ\_v-kernel in U_0(v) at Q) and the primed level U′\_Q with its character ψ′ at T_r..

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-hecke-points-local-conditions"></a>

### Hecke eigen-systems satisfy the lifting data at every place of S

**Theorem.** `R22.1/hecke-points-local-conditions`.

For every 𝒪-algebra map x : 𝕋\_ψ(U)\_𝔪 → 𝒪′ (𝒪′ the integers of a finite extension of E), the specialisation ρ\_x of ρ\_𝔪 has determinant ψχ\_p, is unramified outside S, and satisfies the local condition of the lifting data at each v ∈ S. At v ∈ Σ not above p it is semistable of the form (χ\_pγ\_v ∗; 0 γ\_v) with a fixed unramified γ\_v (KW II Lemma 7.2). At the infinite places it is odd. At v | p it is of type (A) (crystalline of low weight), (B) (weight two) or (C) (weight-two semistable), according to U_v. This identifies the local deformation problem represented at each place.

**Hypotheses.**

- ρ\_𝔪 over 𝕋\_ψ(U)\_𝔪, and the compatibility of π ↦ ρ\_π with the local Langlands correspondence away from p (Carayol, Taylor) and at p (Kisin, Saito, Skinner; KW II Lemma 7.7 and Corollary 7.8), are requested from AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations R19.6.
- The local conditions and their rings are LocalGaloisDeformationRings R08.6 (requested).

**Prerequisites.** [R22.1/minimal-level-data](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-minimal-level-data), `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.6`, `LocalGaloisDeformationRings:R08.6`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.6/hecke-algebra-representation-quaternionic`.

**Proof route.**

- 𝒪′-points of 𝕋\_ψ(U)\_𝔪 correspond to the Hecke eigenforms in S_{k,ψ}(U, 𝒪)\_𝔪, hence to cuspidal π with ρ̄\_π ≅ ρ̄ (Jacquet–Langlands, R18.3).
- At v ∈ Σ: π\_v is a twist of Steinberg by an unramified character, fixed independently of the eigenform (KW II Lemma 7.2, p = 2 via U_v = D_v^×).
- At v | p: U_v maximal compact means π\_v unramified, so ρ\_x|D_v is crystalline of weight k; U_1(v) gives weight two with the tame inertial type; v ∈ Σ above p gives semistable weight two (Corollary 7.8).
- At ∞: ρ\_π is totally odd.

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For type (A) at v | p with k = 2 and π\_v unramified, the specialisation is crystalline with Hodge–Tate weights {0, 1}.
- γ\_v does not depend on the eigenform, which is what makes a single local ring R̄^{□,ψ}\_v work for all points.
- Without local–global compatibility at p, the points would satisfy the condition only on inertia at almost all places, which does not identify the local problem.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), §9.1.1 and the proof of Lemma 9.1, pp. 79–80 — §9.1.1 and the proof of Lemma 9.1, pp. 79–80: the local deformation conditions of the lifting data at each v ∈ S (semistable with fixed γ\_v away from p, odd at infinity, types (A)/(B)/(C) above p), and the check, by local–global compatibility and Lemma 7.7, that every O′-point of 𝕋\_ψ(U)\_𝔪 gives a representation satisfying them.; [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), Lemma 7.2, pp. 60–61 — Lemma 7.2, pp. 60–61: for f in S_{k,ψ}(U, O)\_𝔪 and v ∈ Σ, ρ\_f|D_v has the shape (χ\_pγ\_v ∗; 0 γ\_v) with γ\_v unramified and γ\_v² = ψ\_v, and γ\_v does not depend on f (by the residual representation for p > 2, by U_v = D_v^× for p = 2)..

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-deformation-to-hecke-map"></a>

### The deformation-to-Hecke map

**Construction.** `R22.1/deformation-to-hecke-map`.

There is a continuous local O-algebra homomorphism π:R̄\_S^ψ→T_ψ(U)\_m classifying the Hecke-valued representation, with π(tr ρ\_univ(Frob_v))=T_v away from S. It is unique up to the fixed strict-equivalence identification of that representation. This construction is unframed; its framed base change is a separate target.

**Hypotheses.**

- The factorisation through the local-condition quotient is GlobalGaloisDeformations R04.6/factorization-through-local-conditions, applied to A = 𝕋\_ψ(U)\_𝔪, which is reduced, 𝒪-flat and finite (R18.3).
- The universal property of R^ψ\_S is GlobalGaloisDeformations R04.2.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.1/hecke-points-local-conditions](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-hecke-points-local-conditions), `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.6/factorization-through-local-conditions`, `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.6/trace-subring-universal-representation`, `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.2/universal-deformation-ring`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.6`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.6/hecke-algebra-representation-quaternionic`.

**Proof route.**

- ρ\_𝔪 is a lift of ρ̄ with determinant ψχ\_p, unramified outside S, so the universal property gives π′ : R^ψ\_S → 𝕋\_ψ(U)\_𝔪.
- Its points satisfy the local conditions (hecke-points-local-conditions), so π′ factors through R̄^ψ\_S (R04.6/factorization-through-local-conditions).
- Traces: π(tr ρ̄^univ(Frob_v)) = tr ρ\_𝔪(Frob_v) = T_v, by the Eichler–Shimura relation.

**API and Checks.** Instantiate the coefficient ring, representation, fields, levels and actions specified above. Check the following signatures over those actual objects; no existential witness or conclusion may be added as an input merely to satisfy a Check.

- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.defToHecke` (constructor): π : R̄^ψ\_S →ₐ[𝒪] 𝕋\_ψ(U)\_𝔪.
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.defToHecke_trace` (characterisation): π(tr ρ̄^univ(Frob_v)) = T_v for v ∉ S.
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.defToHecke_unique` (universal-property): Unique with π ∘ ρ̄^univ ≅ ρ\_𝔪.

**Unit-test specifications.**

- `defToHecke_trace_Frob` (characterisation): Traces of Frobenius map to T_v.
- `defToHecke_point` (compatibility): Composing with the point of π gives the lift ρ\_π.
- `defToHecke_not_injective_claim` (non-example): No injectivity is part of the construction.

**Acceptance checks.**

- Composing with an 𝒪′-point x of 𝕋 gives the point of R̄^ψ\_S classifying ρ\_x.
- The map is not asserted injective: R = T is the conclusion of R22.3 and R22.4.
- Without the local-condition factorisation one only gets R^ψ\_S → 𝕋, whose kernel need not contain the local-condition ideal.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), Lemma 9.1, p. 80 — Lemma 9.1, p. 80: the map R^ψ\_S → 𝕋\_ψ(U)\_𝔪 classifying ρ\_𝔪 factors through a surjection R̄^ψ\_S → 𝕋\_ψ(U)\_𝔪 taking the universal representation to ρ\_𝔪, and induces a surjection R̄\_{S,ψ}^□ → 𝕋^□.; [GEE-MLT-2022](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2202.05818v2), §5.6, p. 35 — §5.6, p. 35: the lifting ρ^mod of type S_∅ over 𝕋\_∅ gives, by the universal property, a surjection R^univ_∅ ↠ 𝕋\_∅, surjective because local–global compatibility puts the generating Hecke operators in the image..

**Planet.** Deformation-to-Hecke map.

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-deformation-to-hecke-traces"></a>

### Trace characterization of the Hecke map

**Lemma.** `R22.1/deformation-to-hecke-traces`.

For the minimal-level Hecke map π and every v outside S, π(tr ρ\_univ(Frob_v))=T_v and π(det ρ\_univ(Frob_v))=N(v)ψ(π\_v), with the normalization fixed by the Hecke representation. These identities determine π because the global trace subring is the entire unframed deformation ring.

**Hypotheses.**

- ρ̄ is absolutely irreducible, so the unframed ring is generated topologically by traces (GlobalGaloisDeformations R04.6/trace-subring-universal-representation); T_v and S_v are the Hecke operators at the places v ∉ S, with S_v acting by ψ(π\_v).

**Prerequisites.** [R22.1/deformation-to-hecke-map](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-deformation-to-hecke-map), `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.6/trace-subring-universal-representation`.

**Proof route.**

- The classifying property identifies the specialized universal representation with the fixed Hecke-valued representation. Apply its Frobenius characteristic-polynomial identities outside S.
- The trace-subring theorem says that these traces topologically generate the unframed deformation ring; continuity then gives uniqueness of the map.

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- KW II use arithmetic Frobenius: the characteristic polynomial of ρ\_𝔪(Frob_v) is X² − T_vX + N(v)ψ(π\_v); with geometric Frobenius the eigenvalues are inverted and both coefficients change, so the convention is part of the statement.
- Two continuous maps R̄^ψ\_S → 𝕋 agreeing on all tr ρ\_univ(Frob_v), v ∉ S, are equal, by Chebotarev density and continuity.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), Lemma 9.1 and proof, pp. 79–80 — Lemma 9.1 gives the map R̄^ψ\_S → 𝕋\_ψ(U)\_𝔪 taking the universal representation to ρ\_𝔪, and p. 80 records that ρ\_𝔪 is characterised by the Eichler–Shimura relation X² − T_vX + N(v)ψ(π\_v) for v ∉ S..

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-deformation-to-hecke-surjective"></a>

### Surjectivity of the deformation-to-Hecke map

**Theorem.** `R22.1/deformation-to-hecke-surjective`.

The unframed deformation-to-Hecke map π:R̄\_S^ψ→T_ψ(U)\_m is surjective. Framed surjectivity is established after the framed algebra is constructed, in framed-deformation-to-hecke-map.

**Hypotheses.**

- 𝕋\_ψ(U) is generated over 𝒪 by T_v and S_v for v ∉ S, and S_v acts by the scalar ψ(π\_v) ∈ 𝒪^× (R18.3).

**Prerequisites.** [R22.1/deformation-to-hecke-map](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-deformation-to-hecke-map), [R22.1/deformation-to-hecke-traces](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-deformation-to-hecke-traces).

**Proof route.**

- The image of π contains T_v = π(tr ρ̄^univ(Frob_v)) for all v ∉ S, and 𝒪.
- 𝕋\_ψ(U)\_𝔪 is finite over 𝒪 and the image of a complete local ring is closed, so the image is all of 𝕋\_ψ(U)\_𝔪.
- π^□ is the base change of π along the flat map R̄^ψ\_S → R̄^{□,ψ}\_S, so it is surjective as well.

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- Gee's form: surjective 'because local-global compatibility shows that the Hecke operators generating T∅ are in the image'.
- At Taylor–Wiles level the same argument gives R̄^ψ\_{S∪Q} ↠ 𝕋\_{ψ,Q}(U_Q)\_𝔪 (auxiliary-hecke-algebra).
- If 𝕋 included U_v at a place of S outside the image of the traces, surjectivity could fail; KW II's 𝕋\_ψ(U) uses only v ∉ S.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), proof of Lemma 9.1, p. 81 — Proof of Lemma 9.1, pp. 80–81: the classifying map factors through R̄^ψ\_S since every point satisfies the local conditions, and surjectivity onto 𝕋\_ψ(U)\_𝔪 follows from the Eichler–Shimura relation..

**Planet.** Surjectivity from Frobenius traces.

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-framed-hecke-algebra"></a>

### Framed Hecke algebra

**Construction.** `R22.1/framed-hecke-algebra`.

Fix a framing presentation R̄\_S^{□,ψ}≅R̄\_S^ψ[[y_1,…,y_j]], j=4|S|−1. Define T^□=T⊗\_{R̄\_S^ψ}R̄\_S^{□,ψ}. Because T is a finite, hence finitely presented, R̄\_S^ψ-module over the complete Noetherian base, this ordinary tensor product agrees with the completed tensor and identifies with T[[y_1,…,y_j]].

**Hypotheses.**

- T = 𝕋\_ψ(U)\_𝔪 is finite over O, reduced and O-flat, and receives the surjection from R̄^ψ\_S (deformation-to-hecke-surjective); the framing presentation of R̄\_S^{□,ψ} over R̄^ψ\_S is fixed once (GlobalGaloisDeformations R04.2/framed-unframed-comparison).

**Prerequisites.** [R22.1/deformation-to-hecke-surjective](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-deformation-to-hecke-surjective), `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.2/framed-unframed-comparison`, `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.1/flat-completion-and-finite-module-comparison`.

**Proof route.**

- Use the chosen framed/unframed presentation and the unframed surjection. The finite O-module T is finite over R̄, hence finitely presented because R̄ is Noetherian.
- Apply the finite-module completion comparison to identify T⊗R̄ R̄[[y]] with T[[y]]. The quotient by the variables is T. No tensor–infinite-product interchange is used without finite presentation.

**API and Checks.** Instantiate the coefficient ring, representation, fields, levels and actions specified above. Check the following signatures over those actual objects; no existential witness or conclusion may be added as an input merely to satisfy a Check.

- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.framedHecke` (constructor): The O-algebra T⊗\_R̄ R̄^□ with its specified deformation and Hecke maps.
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.framedHecke_series` (equivalence): T^□≅T[[y_1,…,y_j]] as T-algebras, for the chosen framing presentation and finite presentation of T.
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.framedHecke_augmentation` (compatibility): Quotienting by all framing variables gives T with its original map from R̄.

**Unit-test specifications.**

- `framedHecke_three` (computation): For |S|=1 the ring is T[[y_1,y_2,y_3]].
- `framedHecke_aug` (degenerate): Killing every framing variable recovers T.
- `framedHecke_tensor_limit` (non-example): The interchange of a tensor product with infinite coefficient products requires finite presentation; no unrestricted tensor–power-series identity is asserted.

**Acceptance checks.**

- T^□ is not finite over O when |S| ≥ 1: it has j = 4|S| − 1 free framing variables.
- For a module that is not finitely presented the ordinary tensor with a power-series ring need not be the module of power series; the identification is used only for the finite T.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), Lemma 9.1 and §9.1.1, pp. 79–80 — After Lemma 9.1, KW II define 𝕋^□ = 𝕋 ⊗\_{R̄^ψ\_S} R̄\_S^{□,ψ}, note that R̄\_S^{□,ψ} is formally smooth over R̄^ψ\_S, and deduce that 𝕋^□ is again O-flat and reduced..

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-framed-hecke-module"></a>

### Framed Hecke module

**Construction.** `R22.1/framed-hecke-module`.

For the finite O-free Hecke module M=S_{k,ψ}(U,O)\_m, define M^□=M⊗\_{R̄\_S^ψ}R̄\_S^{□,ψ}. Its commuting T^□-action is induced by the Hecke action and the framing presentation. Finite presentation over the Noetherian deformation base gives M^□≅M[[y_1,…,y_j]], and augmentation gives M.

**Hypotheses.**

- M is finite O-free; T is its faithful Hecke action image. Fix the same framing presentation used to define T^□. All tensor–completion comparisons use finite presentation.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.1/framed-hecke-algebra](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-framed-hecke-algebra), `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.1/flat-completion-and-finite-module-comparison`.

**Proof route.**

- Use the finite-module tensor–completion comparison, then transport the two commuting actions. The action, not just the underlying O-module, is preserved under the augmentation.

**API and Checks.** Instantiate the coefficient ring, representation, fields, levels and actions specified above. Check the following signatures over those actual objects; no existential witness or conclusion may be added as an input merely to satisfy a Check.

- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.framedHeckeModule` (constructor): M^□ with its T^□-action and O[[y]]-scalar action.
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.framedHeckeModule_series` (equivalence): M^□≅M[[y]] as modules with both actions.
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.framedHeckeModule_augmentation` (compatibility): M^□/(y)≅M, T^□/(y)≅T equivariantly.

**Unit-test specifications.**

- `framedModule_rank` (computation): If M≅O^s, then M^□≅O[[y]]^s.
- `framedModule_aug` (degenerate): Augmentation recovers the original Hecke module with its action.
- `framedModule_action` (non-example): Replacing the faithful Hecke action on a nonzero M by the zero action fails the required unital T^□-module compatibility.

**Acceptance checks.**

- |S| = 1: three framing variables.
- M^□/(y_{h+1}, …, y_{h+j}) = S_{k,ψ}(U, 𝒪)\_𝔪.
- 𝕋^□\_𝔪 is not finite over 𝒪: the framing variables are free.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), Lemma 9.1 and §9.1.1, p. 80 — Lemma 9.1 and the note after it, p. 80: 𝕋^□ = 𝕋 ⊗\_{R̄^ψ\_S} R̄\_{S,ψ}^□, with R̄\_{S,ψ}^□ formally smooth over R̄^ψ\_S, so 𝕋^□ is O-flat and reduced; the framed module is the same base change of S_{k,ψ}(U, O)\_𝔪..

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-framed-hecke-properties"></a>

### Faithfulness and freeness after framing

**Lemma.** `R22.1/framed-hecke-properties`.

Assume M≠0 is finite O-free and faithful over its finite reduced O-flat Hecke action image T. For the chosen framing presentation, T^□ is reduced and O-flat, M^□ is faithful over T^□, and M^□ is finite free of rank rank_O M over O[[y_1,…,y_j]]. The faithful flat base extension argument uses the finite presentation of M.

**Hypotheses.**

- M ≠ 0 is finite free over O and faithful over its Hecke action image T, which is finite, reduced and O-flat; the framing presentation is the one used for T^□ and M^□.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.1/framed-hecke-module](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-framed-hecke-module), [R22.1/framed-hecke-algebra](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-framed-hecke-algebra), `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.1/flat-completion-and-finite-module-comparison`.

**Proof route.**

- Use the finite-presentation power-series identifications T^□≅T[[y]] and M^□≅M[[y]]. A basis of the finite O-free module M gives a basis of M^□ over O[[y]].
- A nonzero power series acting on M[[y]] has a first nonzero coefficient, which acts nontrivially on some element of M by faithfulness. Thus the framed action is faithful. Reducedness of T[[y]] and O-flatness follow coefficientwise from reducedness and O-torsion-freeness of T.

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For M = O and T = O the conclusions read: O[[y]] is reduced, O-flat, and O[[y]] acts faithfully on itself.
- Faithfulness of M^□ over T^□ uses faithfulness of M over T: the zero action of T on M would give a non-faithful framed action.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), Lemma 9.1 and §9.1.1, p. 80 — p. 80, after Lemma 9.1: since R̄\_S^{□,ψ} is formally smooth over R̄^ψ\_S and 𝕋 is O-flat and reduced, 𝕋^□ is O-flat and reduced; the framed module is the same base change of the Hecke module..

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-framed-deformation-to-hecke-map"></a>

### Framed deformation-to-Hecke map

**Construction.** `R22.1/framed-deformation-to-hecke-map`.

Define π^□:R̄\_S^{□,ψ}→T^□ by r↦1⊗r. It is the base change of π, is surjective, and the chosen framing presentations identify it with coefficientwise π on power series.

**Hypotheses.**

- The unframed π : R̄^ψ\_S ↠ T and the framing presentation R̄\_S^{□,ψ} ≅ R̄^ψ\_S[[y_1,…,y_j]] are fixed; π^□ is defined on the framed ring by base change, not by a choice of images of the framing variables.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.1/framed-hecke-algebra](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-framed-hecke-algebra), [R22.1/deformation-to-hecke-surjective](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-deformation-to-hecke-surjective).

**Proof route.**

- Base change the unframed surjective map R̄→T along R̄→R̄^□. Right exactness of tensor gives surjectivity.
- The selected power-series presentations identify this map with coefficientwise π. Taking the quotient by the variables recovers π and proves the augmentation square.

**API and Checks.** Instantiate the coefficient ring, representation, fields, levels and actions specified above. Check the following signatures over those actual objects; no existential witness or conclusion may be added as an input merely to satisfy a Check.

- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.defToHeckeFramed` (constructor): The continuous O-algebra map r↦1⊗r.
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.defToHeckeFramed_coeff` (compatibility): Its restriction to R̄ is π followed by T→T^□.
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.defToHeckeFramed_aug` (compatibility): Its quotient by all framing variables is π.

**Unit-test specifications.**

- `framedMap_variable` (computation): Each chosen framing variable maps to the matching variable in T[[y]].
- `framedMap_aug` (degenerate): At augmentation the map is the unframed map.
- `framedMap_not_aug` (non-example): For j>0 and T≠0, sending all framing variables to zero does not give the specified base-change map.

**Acceptance checks.**

- Right exactness: the base change of a surjective ring map is surjective, so π^□ is onto T^□.
- Composing π^□ with the quotient by (y_{h+1},…,y_{h+j}) recovers π.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), §9.1.1 after Lemma 9.1, p. 80 — Lemma 9.1, second sentence: with 𝕋^□ = 𝕋 ⊗\_{R̄^ψ\_S} R̄\_S^{□,ψ}, π induces a surjection R̄\_S^{□,ψ} → 𝕋^□, again denoted π..

## R22.2

Auxiliary levels, action image, branch ideal, deformation map, eigencharacter identities, surjectivity and module-system control are planned here; the Hensel root and localized Δ\_Q-freeness with coinvariant control (KW II Corollary 7.5) are HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves R18.3/tw-localised-control, applied at the actual Taylor–Wiles primes.

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-auxiliary-level-groups"></a>

### Auxiliary levels at Taylor–Wiles places

**Definition.** `R22.2/auxiliary-level-groups`.

For minimal-level data and the Global R04.5/R04.6 Taylor–Wiles datum Q disjoint from S, specialize the existing R18.3/tw-level construction. Use the supplied isotropy exponent bound N, Δ′\_v the maximal p-quotient of k(v)×, Δ\_v=Δ′\_v/Δ′\_v[N] and its product Δ\_Q. The actual arithmetic levels have (U_Q^0)\_v=U_0(v) and (U_Q)\_v the kernel of a/d→Δ\_v, and are U away from Q. Record their identification U_Q^0/U_Q≅Δ\_Q, the chosen uniformizers and root labels, and the common coefficient lattice/character. The generic level and group-quotient constructions belong to R18.3; this target assembles them for this lifting problem.

**Hypotheses.**

- The finiteness of the isotropy groups and the bound on the exponent of their Sylow p-subgroups are KW II §7.2 (R18.3, via R18.6). For p ≥ 5 unramified in F they have order prime to p (Gee §5.3), so N is prime to p and Δ\_v = Δ′\_v.
- Quotienting by the N-torsion is what makes characters of Δ\_Q kill the isotropy groups; the congruence N(v) ≡ 1 mod p^n alone does not.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.1/minimal-level-data](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-minimal-level-data), `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.5/taylor-wiles-datum`, `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:R18.6`, `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:R18.3/tw-level`, `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:R18.3/isotropy-exponent`, `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:R18.3/tw-stabilisers`.

**Proof route.**

- U_Q is normal in U^0_Q, and a ↦ ad^{−1} induces U^0_Q/U_Q ≅ ∏\_v Δ\_v.
- Every character of Δ\_v, viewed on Δ′\_v, kills the N-torsion, so it is an N-th power.

**API and Checks.** Instantiate the coefficient ring, representation, fields, levels and actions specified above. Check the following signatures over those actual objects; no existential witness or conclusion may be added as an input merely to satisfy a Check.

- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.auxLevel` (constructor): U_Q ⊆ U^0_Q ⊆ U for a Taylor–Wiles datum Q.
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.auxDelta` (constructor): Δ\_Q = ∏\_v Δ′\_v/(N-torsion).
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.auxLevel_quotient` (equivalence): U^0_Q/U_Q ≃ Δ\_Q.
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.auxDelta_char_pow` (characterisation): Under the fixed coefficient extension containing the needed roots of unity, the supplier’s Δ\_Q characters are N-th powers on Δ′\_Q, for these arithmetic levels.

**Unit-test specifications.**

- `auxLevel_empty` (degenerate): Q = ∅ gives U.
- `auxLevel_quotient` (characterisation): U^0_Q/U_Q ≅ Δ\_Q.
- `auxDelta_needs_quotient` (non-example): With Δ′\_v in place of Δ\_v, characters need not kill p-torsion isotropy.

**Acceptance checks.**

- Q = ∅: U_Q = U^0_Q = U and Δ\_∅ = 1.
- For p ≥ 5 unramified (Gee), N is prime to p and Δ\_v is the full p-part of k(v)^×.
- Using Δ′\_v when the isotropy groups have p-torsion (possible for p = 2, 3) breaks the freeness of Lemma 7.4: a character of Δ′\_v need not kill the isotropy.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), §7.4, p. 63 — §7.4, p. 63: N is the least common multiple of the exponents of the Sylow p-subgroups of the isotropy groups; Δ′\_v is the p-quotient of k_v^×, Δ\_v its quotient by the N-torsion; U_Q ⊂ U′\_Q are defined at v ∈ Q by ad^{−1} ↦ 1 in Δ\_v and by upper-triangularity mod π\_v, with U′\_Q/U_Q ≅ Δ\_Q.; [GEE-MLT-2022](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2202.05818v2), §5.6, p. 35 — §5.6, p. 35: Gee's U_{Q,v} is the subgroup of U_0(v) with a/d mod v mapping to 1 in Δ\_v, Δ\_v the maximal p-quotient of k(v)^×..

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-auxiliary-hecke-algebra"></a>

### Auxiliary Hecke action image

**Construction.** `R22.2/auxiliary-hecke-algebra`.

For the specified auxiliary level U_Q, let T_{ψ,Q}(U_Q) be the O-subalgebra of End_O S_{k,ψ}(U_Q,O) generated by T_v,S_v for v outside S∪Q, the uniformizer-dependent U_v at v∈Q, and diamonds for Δ\_Q. This target constructs the action image before choosing a residual branch or constructing a deformation map.

**Hypotheses.**

- Fix the level, central character, coefficient lattice and uniformizers. The diamond action factors through Δ\_Q; the global deformation ring still carries the larger Δ′\_Q action.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.2/auxiliary-level-groups](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-auxiliary-level-groups), `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:R18.6`.

**Proof route.**

- Take the algebra generated by the specified Hecke operators on the finite O-module. The branch localization, eigenvalue identities and comparison with the smaller Hecke algebra are separate targets.

**API and Checks.** Instantiate the coefficient ring, representation, fields, levels and actions specified above. Check the following signatures over those actual objects; no existential witness or conclusion may be added as an input merely to satisfy a Check.

- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.auxHecke` (constructor): The generated O-subalgebra of End_O S(U_Q,O).
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.auxHecke_action_faithful` (characterisation): The tautological action is faithful because this is its action image.
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.auxHecke_diamonds` (compatibility): Diamonds give O[Δ\_Q]→T_{ψ,Q}(U_Q), compatible with right translation.

**Unit-test specifications.**

- `auxHecke_empty` (degenerate): For Q empty this is the original Hecke algebra.
- `auxHecke_scalar` (compatibility): A scalar central operator acts by the fixed central character.
- `auxHecke_delta_quotient` (non-example): An element of Δ′\_Q[N] acts trivially after passage to Δ\_Q; retaining an independent nontrivial diamond for it gives the wrong algebra.

**Acceptance checks.**

- Q = ∅ recovers 𝕋\_ψ(U)\_𝔪 and deformation-to-hecke-map.
- U_v ↦ A_v under the map to level U (delta-freeness-at-taylor-wiles-level), not U_v ↦ B_v.
- With equal residual eigenvalues the simple-root Hensel argument and separation into two residual eigenlines fail; this does not assert that a maximal ideal cannot exist.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), §7.4 before Corollary 7.5, p. 65; §9.1.1, p. 80 — §7.4, pp. 63–65: the extended Hecke algebra 𝕋\_{ψ,Q}(U_Q) generated over 𝕋\_ψ(U_Q) by U_v, S_v (v ∈ Q) and Δ\_Q, and the pull-back of 𝔪 by declaring U_v − α̃\_v ∈ 𝔪; §9.1.1, p. 80, uses it at level U_{Q_n}.; [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), Remark after Lemma 9.1, p. 81 — Remark after Lemma 9.1, p. 81: the proof of Lemma 9.1 also gives surjectivity of R̄^ψ\_{S∪Q_n} → 𝕋\_{ψ,Q_n}(U_{Q_n})\_𝔪..


<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-auxiliary-maximal-ideal"></a>

### Auxiliary residual branch ideal

**Construction.** `R22.2/auxiliary-maximal-ideal`.

Let m^away be the ideal of the algebra generated by away-from-Q operators specifying the residual eigensystem. Choose α\_v at every v∈Q and any coefficient representative α̃\_v. Define m_Q=(m^away,U_v−α̃\_v for v∈Q) in T_{ψ,Q}(U_Q), and take its complete local factor. The ideal is independent of all representatives of the same α\_v; choosing β\_v selects the other branch.

**Hypotheses.**

- For each v ∈ Q the residual Frobenius eigenvalues α\_v ≠ β\_v are fixed and N(v) ≡ 1 mod p; the residual eigensystem away from Q is the non-Eisenstein 𝔪 of the minimal level.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.2/auxiliary-hecke-algebra](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-auxiliary-hecke-algebra), `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:R18.3/tw-localised-control`, `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:R18.3/iwahori-selected-root`.

**Proof route.**

- Apply the supplied selected-root Iwahori comparison to the distinct residual eigenvalues to obtain the nonzero residual eigenspace with U_v=α\_v. Its Hecke character defines the required maximal ideal.
- Two coefficient representatives differ by the coefficient maximal ideal, already in m^away. They generate the same ideal. The other residual root defines a different residual character; it is a different branch, rather than another lift of the same root.

**API and Checks.** Instantiate the coefficient ring, representation, fields, levels and actions specified above. Check the following signatures over those actual objects; no existential witness or conclusion may be added as an input merely to satisfy a Check.

- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.auxMaxIdeal` (constructor): The branch ideal (m^away,U_v−α̃\_v:v∈Q).
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.auxMaxIdeal_lift_independent` (compatibility): Replacing α̃\_v by a congruent representative leaves the ideal and its local factor unchanged. Proved by [R22.2/auxiliary-maximal-ideal](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-auxiliary-maximal-ideal).
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.auxMaxIdeal_residue` (characterisation): In the residue field U_v acts by the chosen α\_v and the away-from-Q operators by the fixed residual eigensystem.

**Unit-test specifications.**

- `auxIdeal_empty` (degenerate): When Q is empty the branch ideal is m.
- `auxIdeal_representative` (computation): Changing α̃\_v to α̃\_v+πc adds an element already in m^away and gives the same ideal.
- `auxIdeal_other_root` (non-example): If α\_v≠β\_v, the two branch ideals cannot be identified: their U_v residues differ.

**Acceptance checks.**

- Q = ∅ gives back 𝔪.
- Choosing β\_v instead of α\_v at one place gives a different maximal ideal: the residues of U_v differ.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), §7.4, pp. 64–65; Lemma 9.1, p. 80 — §7.4, before Corollary 7.5, pp. 64–65: by Hensel the Hecke polynomial splits as (X − A_v)(X − B_v) in 𝕋\_ψ(U)\_𝔪, and 𝔪 is pulled back to the auxiliary Hecke algebra by declaring U_v − α̃\_v ∈ 𝔪 for a lift α̃\_v of α\_v (citing part 2 of Lemma 1.6 of their reference [59]); Lemma 9.1 uses the same pull-back at level U_{Q_n}..

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-delta-freeness-at-taylor-wiles-level"></a>

### Freeness at the arithmetic Taylor–Wiles levels

**Theorem.** `R22.2/delta-freeness-at-taylor-wiles-level`.

At the Taylor–Wiles data Q of GlobalGaloisDeformations R04.5 and the selected branch m_Q, S_{k,ψ}(U_Q,O)\_{m_Q} is finite free over O[Δ\_Q] of rank s = rank_O S_{k,ψ}(U,O)\_m, and its Δ\_Q-coinvariants are S_{k,ψ}(U,O)\_m through ξ\_v(f) = A_v f − (1 0; 0 π\_v)f. This is HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves R18.3/tw-localised-control applied at these primes; what is checked here is that the R04.5 primes satisfy its hypotheses (N(v) ≡ 1 mod p^n, two distinct residual Frobenius eigenvalues, the isotropy quotient Δ\_Q of R22.2/auxiliary-level-groups).

**Hypotheses.**

- The localized freeness and coinvariant control, with the Hensel root A_v and the Steinberg exclusion through local–global compatibility, are HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves R18.3/tw-localised-control (KW II Corollary 7.5); geometric freeness is R18.3/tw-freeness (Lemma 7.4).
- The congruence N(v) ≡ 1 mod p^n alone does not give freeness: the isotropy quotient of auxiliary-level-groups is used.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.2/auxiliary-level-groups](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-auxiliary-level-groups), [R22.2/auxiliary-hecke-algebra](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-auxiliary-hecke-algebra), `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:R18.6`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.6`, `mathlib:Module.Free`, `mathlib:MonoidAlgebra`, [R22.2/auxiliary-maximal-ideal](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-auxiliary-maximal-ideal), `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:R18.3/tw-localised-control`, `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:R18.3/iwahori-selected-root`, `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:R18.3/tw-freeness`.

**Proof route.**

- The R04.5 Taylor–Wiles primes are prime to S, have N(v) ≡ 1 mod p^n and distinct residual Frobenius eigenvalues α\_v ≠ β\_v; the branch m_Q contains U_v − α̃\_v (auxiliary-maximal-ideal).
- With Δ\_Q = Δ′\_Q/Δ′\_Q[N] as in auxiliary-level-groups, these are the hypotheses of R18.3/tw-localised-control, which gives freeness of rank s and the coinvariant isomorphism through ξ\_v.

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- Q = ∅: the statement is the tautology S(U)\_𝔪 = S(U)\_𝔪.
- The rank over 𝒪[Δ\_Q] does not depend on Q or n, which is what patching needs.
- Gee Proposition 5.9 is the same control statement, ∏\_{v∈Q}(U_ϖ\_v − B_v) : S_∅ ≅ S(U_{Q,0}, 𝒪)\_{𝔪\_Q}, with the other eigenvalue B_v.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), Corollary 7.5 and its proof, pp. 65–66 — Corollary 7.5 and its proof, pp. 65–66: S_{k,ψ}(U_Q, O)\_𝔪 is free over O[Δ\_Q] with coinvariants S_{k,ψ}(U, O)\_𝔪; the comparison through the ξ\_v is an isomorphism after inverting p because no form Steinberg at Q gives ρ̄ (N(v) ≡ 1 mod p, distinct eigenvalues), and injective mod λ because the degeneracy maps have Eisenstein kernel (Lemma 7.1).; [GEE-MLT-2022](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2202.05818v2), Proposition 5.8(2) and Proposition 5.9, pp. 36–37 — Propositions 5.8(2) and 5.9, pp. 36–37: S_Q is finite free over O[Δ\_Q], and ∏\_{v∈Q}(U_{ϖ\_v} − B_v) is an isomorphism S_∅ ≅ S(U_{Q,0}, O)\_{𝔪\_Q}, proved by checking it after ⊗L and modulo λ..

**Planet.** Freeness over Δ\_Q.

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-deformation-to-auxiliary-hecke-map"></a>

### Auxiliary deformation-to-Hecke map

**Construction.** `R22.2/deformation-to-auxiliary-hecke-map`.

There is a continuous local O-algebra map π\_Q:R̄\_{S∪Q}^ψ→T_{ψ,Q}(U_Q)\_{m_Q} classifying the Hecke representation with the prescribed local conditions and labelled inertia character γ\_{α\_v}. The ring receives O[Δ′\_Q]; its Hecke image factors through the module diamonds for Δ\_Q.

**Hypotheses.**

- The Hecke algebra 𝕋\_{ψ,Q}(U_Q)\_{𝔪\_Q} is finite, reduced and O-flat, and its characteristic-zero points satisfy the lifting data at S and are unramified-principal-series with a tame inertia character at each v ∈ Q (local–global compatibility, AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations R19.6).

**Prerequisites.** [R22.2/auxiliary-maximal-ideal](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-auxiliary-maximal-ideal), [R22.1/hecke-points-local-conditions](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-hecke-points-local-conditions), `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.6/factorization-through-local-conditions`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.6/hecke-algebra-representation-quaternionic`.

**Proof route.**

- The localized Hecke-valued representation has the specified determinant and residual representation. Check its characteristic-zero points using local–global compatibility, including the selected tame inertia line at every v∈Q.
- Apply the global universal property and local-condition factorization for a finite reduced O-flat Hecke algebra. The labelled line identifies the Δ′\_Q-character. Comparison with module diamonds is proved separately by auxiliary-eigenvalue-compatibility.

**API and Checks.** Instantiate the coefficient ring, representation, fields, levels and actions specified above. Check the following signatures over those actual objects; no existential witness or conclusion may be added as an input merely to satisfy a Check.

- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.defToAuxHecke` (constructor): The continuous local O-algebra homomorphism π\_Q.
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.defToAuxHecke_trace` (characterisation): π\_Q(tr ρ\_univ(Frob_w))=T_w for w outside S∪Q. Proved by [R22.2/deformation-to-auxiliary-hecke-map](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-deformation-to-auxiliary-hecke-map).
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.defToAuxHecke_universal` (universal-property): Pullback of the Hecke representation is the universal deformation with its labelled local conditions.

**Unit-test specifications.**

- `auxMap_empty` (degenerate): For Q empty π\_Q is π.
- `auxMap_trace` (compatibility): Every unramified Frobenius trace has the specified T_w image.
- `auxMap_wrong_branch` (non-example): Labelling γ by β\_v while retaining the α\_v branch is incompatible with the selected residual line.

**Acceptance checks.**

- Q = ∅ recovers deformation-to-hecke-map.
- At p = 2 the map is also compatible with the twisting actions of Hom(G_{Q_n,2}, O^×) (dyadic-twists-of-forms).

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), Lemma 9.1 and proof, pp. 79–81 — p. 80, after Lemma 9.1: there is a map R̄^ψ\_{S∪Q_n} → 𝕋\_{ψ,Q_n}(U_{Q_n})\_𝔪, compatible with the O[Δ′\_{Q_n}]-action (and for p = 2 with the twisting actions), characterised by tr ρ\_univ(Frob_v) ↦ T_v for almost all v; p. 81 proves it by the argument for Lemma 9.1..

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-auxiliary-eigenvalue-compatibility"></a>

### Auxiliary eigencharacter compatibility

**Lemma.** `R22.2/auxiliary-eigenvalue-compatibility`.

For v∈Q, the map π\_Q sends γ\_{α\_v}(π\_v) to U_v on the selected branch and sends its tame inertia character to the matching diamond operator. These are equalities in the complete local Hecke algebra. The arbitrary representative α̃\_v is not asserted to equal U_v.

**Hypotheses.**

- The Hecke algebra at level U_Q is reduced and O-flat, so an identity holds in it as soon as it holds at every characteristic-zero point; at each point local–global compatibility at v ∈ Q is available (AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations R19.6).

**Prerequisites.** [R22.2/deformation-to-auxiliary-hecke-map](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-deformation-to-auxiliary-hecke-map), `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.5/taylor-wiles-inertia-action`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.6/hecke-algebra-representation-quaternionic`.

**Proof route.**
