- At every characteristic-zero Hecke point, local–global compatibility identifies the selected inertia/Frobenius line with the U_v branch reducing to α\_v.
- Compare the resulting U_v and tame-character functions in the finite reduced O-flat localized Hecke algebra. Its embedding in the product of characteristic-zero points gives equality in that algebra, not merely equality after reduction.

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The operator U_v depends on the uniformiser π\_v; the identity γ\_{α\_v}(π\_v) ↦ U_v is stated for that same π\_v.
- An arbitrary lift α̃\_v of α\_v is not U_v; only its reduction is fixed.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), proof of Lemma 9.1, pp. 80–81 — Proof of Lemma 9.1, p. 81: local–global compatibility gives that the map to 𝕋\_{ψ,Q_n}(U_{Q_n})\_𝔪 takes γ\_{α\_v}(π\_v) to U_v for v ∈ Q_n (notation of Proposition 5.11), noting that U_v depends on π\_v..

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-dyadic-twists-of-forms"></a>

### Dyadic Hecke twists agree with deformation twists

**Comparison.** `R22.2/dyadic-twists-of-forms`.

For p=2 and the actual auxiliary levels, identify the quadratic class-field characters used in Global R04.6 with finite-idelic characters trivial at infinity and on Nrd U_Q. Import the norm twist and its T_v,U_v,S_v,diamond formulas from Hilbert R18.3. The resulting semilinear action on the localized Hecke module agrees under π\_Q with the deformation-ring twisting action: Frobenius traces acquire χ(π\_v), diamonds acquire χ, and framing variables are fixed. The character is quadratic, so it preserves the central character; χ≡1 modulo the coefficient maximal ideal preserves m_Q.

**Hypotheses.**

- The twist f ↦ f_χ and Proposition 7.6 on quaternionic forms are R18.3's 'actual dyadic twisting input' (RS-23), requested through R18.6. This node is the compatibility with the deformation side used in KW II §9.
- χ is split at the infinite places, so it defines a character of (𝔸\_F^∞)^×.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.2/auxiliary-hecke-algebra](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-auxiliary-hecke-algebra), `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.4/twisting-action`, `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.5/taylor-wiles-inertia-action`, `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.6/dyadic-patching-data`, `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:R18.6`, [R22.2/deformation-to-auxiliary-hecke-map](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-deformation-to-auxiliary-hecke-map), [R22.2/auxiliary-eigenvalue-compatibility](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-auxiliary-eigenvalue-compatibility), `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:R18.3/dyadic-norm-twist`, `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:R18.3/dyadic-hecke-twist`, `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:R18.3/dyadic-level-norm-squares`.

**Proof route.**

- Apply R18.3’s exported order-two idele-character twist and Hecke formulas (Proposition 7.6), after identifying the R04.5 class-field-theory characters with the finite-idelic characters trivial at infinity and unramified outside Q. Their invariance/formula proof stays with R18.3.
- Since χ ≡ 1 mod 𝔪, the action preserves 𝔪. Compatibility with the R-side twists holds because both sides multiply Frobenius traces by χ(π\_v) and act on Δ by χ (Lemma 5.12).

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- χ trivial: the identity action.
- (f|⟨h⟩)\_χ = χ^{−1}(h)f_χ|⟨h⟩: the diamond operators are twisted by χ^{−1}, matching a_χ ∘ δ = χ(δ)(δ ∘ a_χ).
- For p odd, order-2 characters are not residually trivial, so there is no such action on the localisation.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), §7.5, Proposition 7.6 and the following paragraph, pp. 66–67 — §7.5, Proposition 7.6 and the paragraph after it, pp. 66–67: for p = 2 and χ of order 2 of G_{n,2}, split at infinity, f ↦ f_χ(g) = f(g)χ(Nm(g)) preserves S_{k,ψ}(U_{Q_n}, O), and Proposition 7.6 gives its effect on T_v, U_v, S_v and the diamonds.; [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), proof of Lemma 9.1, p. 81 — Compatibility with the deformation side..

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-auxiliary-map-surjective"></a>

### Surjectivity at auxiliary level

**Lemma.** `R22.2/auxiliary-map-surjective`.

The auxiliary deformation-to-Hecke map π\_Q is surjective. Away-from-Q traces, the selected U_v and the diamond characters generate the localized Hecke algebra and all lie in its image.

**Hypotheses.**

- The auxiliary Hecke algebra is generated over O by T_w, S_w (w ∉ S ∪ Q), U_v and the diamond operators (v ∈ Q), and is complete local after localisation at 𝔪\_Q.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.2/deformation-to-auxiliary-hecke-map](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-deformation-to-auxiliary-hecke-map), [R22.2/auxiliary-eigenvalue-compatibility](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-auxiliary-eigenvalue-compatibility), [R22.2/auxiliary-hecke-algebra](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-auxiliary-hecke-algebra), `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.6/trace-subring-universal-representation`.

**Proof route.**

- Away-from-Q Hecke operators are images of universal Frobenius traces; the determinant operators are prescribed coefficient scalars.
- The eigenvalue-compatibility theorem places every U_v and diamond generator in the image. These generators topologically generate the complete local action algebra, so the continuous map is surjective.

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- Q = ∅: this is deformation-to-hecke-surjective.
- Without the identity γ\_{α\_v}(π\_v) ↦ U_v the operators U_v need not lie in the image of the trace map.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), Lemma 9.1 and following remark, pp. 80–81 — Remark after Lemma 9.1, p. 81: the proof of Lemma 9.1 also gives surjectivity of R̄^ψ\_{S∪Q_n} → 𝕋\_{ψ,Q_n}(U_{Q_n})\_𝔪..

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-auxiliary-hecke-comparison"></a>

### Comparison with the trace-generated Hecke algebra

**Lemma.** `R22.2/auxiliary-hecke-comparison`.

The natural inclusion T_ψ(U_Q)\_m→T_{ψ,Q}(U_Q)\_{m_Q} is an isomorphism. The chosen branch is understood on the right. The trace-generated algebra on the left contains the traces of the reconstructed representation; residual absolute irreducibility allows descent of that representation and forces the extra U_v and diamond operators into the image.

**Hypotheses.**

- ρ̄ is absolutely irreducible, so a representation whose traces lie in a closed subalgebra is defined over that subalgebra (Carayol, GlobalGaloisDeformations R04.2/carayol-trace-theorem).

**Prerequisites.** [R22.2/auxiliary-map-surjective](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-auxiliary-map-surjective), [R22.2/auxiliary-eigenvalue-compatibility](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-auxiliary-eigenvalue-compatibility), `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.2/carayol-trace-theorem`.

**Proof route.**

- Work in the selected local factor of the enlarged action image. Its Hecke representation has traces in the closed away-from-Q trace algebra and absolutely irreducible residual representation.
- Carayol trace descent gives a representation over that smaller algebra. Its labelled local eigenspaces recover U_v and the diamond characters there by the same distinct-root and inertia argument. Thus the inclusion contains every extra generator and is surjective as well as injective.

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- Q = ∅: both algebras are 𝕋\_ψ(U)\_𝔪.
- The comparison is at the selected branch 𝔪\_Q; at the other branch (β\_v instead of α\_v) it is a different local factor.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), remark following Lemma 9.1, p. 81 — Remark after Lemma 9.1, p. 81: the traces of the representation into 𝕋\_{ψ,Q_n}(U_{Q_n})\_𝔪 lie in the image of 𝕋\_ψ(U_{Q_n}), so by Carayol's Théorème 2 it is defined over that image, and the natural map 𝕋\_ψ(U_{Q_n})\_𝔪 → 𝕋\_{ψ,Q_n}(U_{Q_n})\_𝔪 is bijective..


<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-delta-actions-agree"></a>

### The diamond and Galois actions of Δ\_Q agree

**Theorem.** `R22.2/delta-actions-agree`.

On S_Q := S_{k,ψ}(U_Q, 𝒪)\_{𝔪\_Q} the two actions of Δ\_Q agree: the diamond action h ↦ ⟨h⟩ = (h̃ 0; 0 1), and the action through Δ\_Q → (R̄^ψ\_{S∪Q})^× → 𝕋\_{ψ,Q}(U_Q)\_𝔪 given by ∏\_v γ\_{α\_v} (the character χ\_α ∘ Art). Equivalently, ⟨h⟩ acts on the U_v-eigenline whose actual characteristic-zero eigenvalue reduces to α\_v through the character of π\_v lifting α\_v.

**Hypotheses.**

- The needed local–global compatibility at v ∈ Q (π\_v ≅ χ₁ × χ₂ tamely ramified principal series, with eigenvalues of ρ\_π(Frob_v) equal to #k(v)^{1/2}χ\_i(ϖ\_v)) is AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations R19.6.
- The lift α̃\_v defining 𝔪\_Q is arbitrary and is not asserted to equal the actual U_v-eigenvalue (KW II p. 65; Gee Proposition 5.8, p. 36).

**Prerequisites.** [R22.2/auxiliary-hecke-algebra](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-auxiliary-hecke-algebra), `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.5/taylor-wiles-inertia-action`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.6`, [R22.2/auxiliary-eigenvalue-compatibility](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-auxiliary-eigenvalue-compatibility), `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:R18.3/tw-localised-control`.

**Proof route.**

- A point θ of 𝕋\_Q gives π with π\_v^{U_{Q,v}} ≠ 0, so π\_v is a subquotient of χ₁ × χ₂ with χ\_i tamely ramified.
- (χ₁ × χ₂)^{U_{Q,v}} = ℂφ₁ ⊕ ℂφ\_w, with U_ϖ φ\_w = #k(v)^{1/2}χ₂(ϖ)φ\_w and U_ϖ φ₁ = #k(v)^{1/2}χ₁(ϖ)φ₁ + Xφ\_w.
- αv ≠ βv forces χ₁/χ₂ ≠ |·|^{±1}, so π\_v = χ₁ × χ₂ is irreducible and one may take χ₂(ϖ) ↔ α\_v. The U_v-eigenline with eigenvalue #k(v)^{1/2}χ₂(π\_v) reducing to α\_v is ℂφ\_w, on which (δ 0; 0 1) acts by χ₂(δ) = χ\_α ∘ Art (Gee Proposition 5.8(1)).

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- Q = ∅: nothing to check.
- If one used the β\_v-eigenvalue, the diamond action would match γ\_{β\_v} = γ\_{α\_v}^{−1} on inertia instead.
- The agreement is what lets the patched Hecke module be a module over the patched deformation ring compatibly with 𝒪⟦y_1, …, y_h⟧.

**Sources.** [GEE-MLT-2022](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2202.05818v2), Proposition 5.8(1) and its proof, p. 36 — The statement and the principal-series computation.; [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), Lemma 9.1 (compatibility with 𝒪[Δ′\_{Q_n}]), p. 80 — p. 80, after Lemma 9.1: the map R̄^ψ\_{S∪Q_n} → 𝕋\_{ψ,Q_n}(U_{Q_n})\_𝔪 is compatible with the O[Δ′\_{Q_n}]-actions on both sides (and for p = 2 with the twisting actions)..

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-taylor-wiles-module-system"></a>

### The system of Hecke modules at Taylor–Wiles levels

**Construction.** `R22.2/taylor-wiles-module-system`.

For the Taylor–Wiles data Q_n of GlobalGaloisDeformations R04.6/taylor-wiles-deformation-system, M_n := S_{k,ψ}(U_{Q_n}, 𝒪)\_𝔪 ⊗\_{R̄^ψ\_{S∪Q_n}} R̄^{□,ψ}\_{S∪Q_n} is an 𝒪[Δ\_{Q_n}]⟦y_{h+1}, …, y_{h+j}⟧-module. It is finite free, of rank equal to the 𝒪-rank s of S_{k,ψ}(U, 𝒪)\_𝔪, independent of n. Its (Δ\_{Q_n}, y)-coinvariants are S_{k,ψ}(U, 𝒪)\_𝔪, and the R̄^{□,ψ}\_{S∪Q_n}-action is compatible with R04.6's 𝒪⟦y_1, …, y_{h+j}⟧-structure. This is the finite-level module input to arithmetic patching.

**Hypotheses.**

- The patching of the system (M_∞, R_∞) is R22.3, with DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra R03.5 and R03.6.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.2/delta-freeness-at-taylor-wiles-level](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-delta-freeness-at-taylor-wiles-level), [R22.2/delta-actions-agree](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-delta-actions-agree), [R22.1/framed-hecke-module](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-framed-hecke-module), `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.6/taylor-wiles-deformation-system`, [R22.1/framed-hecke-properties](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-framed-hecke-properties), [R22.1/framed-deformation-to-hecke-map](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-framed-deformation-to-hecke-map), [R22.2/auxiliary-eigenvalue-compatibility](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-auxiliary-eigenvalue-compatibility), `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:R18.3/tw-localised-control`.

**Proof route.**

- Freeness over 𝒪[Δ\_{Q_n}] with the right rank and coinvariants is delta-freeness-at-taylor-wiles-level; tensoring with the framing variables keeps it free.
- Compatibility of the two 𝒪[Δ]-actions is delta-actions-agree; the y-structure on the ring side is R04.6.

**API and Checks.** Instantiate the coefficient ring, representation, fields, levels and actions specified above. Check the following signatures over those actual objects; no existential witness or conclusion may be added as an input merely to satisfy a Check.

- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.twModule` (constructor): M_n = S(U_{Q_n})\_𝔪 ⊗ R̄^{□,ψ}\_{S∪Q_n}.
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.twModule_free` (characterisation): Finite free over 𝒪[Δ\_{Q_n}]⟦y⟧ of rank s. Proved by [R22.2/module-system-control](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-module-system-control).
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.twModule_coinv` (compatibility): Coinvariants ≅ S(U)\_𝔪. Proved by [R22.2/module-system-control](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-module-system-control).

**Unit-test specifications.**

- `twModule_empty` (degenerate): Q = ∅ gives the framed module at level U.
- `twModule_rank_const` (characterisation): The rank does not depend on n.
- `twModule_not_free_without_isotropy` (non-example): With Δ′ instead of Δ and p-torsion isotropy, freeness can fail.

**Acceptance checks.**

- Q_n = ∅: the system is the framed module of framed-hecke-module.
- The annihilator 𝔟\_n of M_n in 𝒪⟦y⟧ lies in ((1 + y_i)^{p^{n−a}} − 1)\_{i≤h} (KW II §9.1.3, with 2^a > N for p = 2).
- Without freeness over 𝒪[Δ], the patched module would not be finite free over 𝒪⟦y⟧, and the depth argument of R22.3 fails.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), proof of Proposition 9.2, p. 82 — Proof of Proposition 9.2, p. 82: the modules S_{k,ψ}(U_{Q_n}, O)\_𝔪 ⊗ R̄^{□,ψ}\_{S∪Q_n} are O[Δ\_{Q_n}][[y_{h+1},…,y_{h+j}]]-modules, free over O[Δ\_{Q_n}] of rank independent of n by Corollary 7.5, with coinvariants S_{k,ψ}(U, O)\_𝔪..

**Planet.** Taylor–Wiles module system.

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-module-system-control"></a>

### Freeness and specialization of the module system

**Lemma.** `R22.2/module-system-control`.

Let R_n and M_n be the framed deformation and Hecke-module systems with one fixed framing choice. M_n is free of rank s over O[Δ\_{Q_n}][[y_{h+1},…,y_{h+j}]], its coefficient action is the restriction of R_n→End(M_n), and M_n/(y_1,…,y_h)≅M^□. Killing all y variables gives M. The rank and every comparison are independent of n.

**Hypotheses.**

- One framing presentation is fixed for all n; the Δ\_{Q_n}-module structure comes from the diamond operators and agrees with the Δ′\_{Q_n}-action on the ring side through delta-actions-agree.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.2/taylor-wiles-module-system](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-taylor-wiles-module-system), `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:R18.3/tw-localised-control`, [R22.2/delta-actions-agree](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-delta-actions-agree), [R22.1/framed-hecke-properties](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-framed-hecke-properties).

**Proof route.**

- Base change the finite free O[Δ\_Q]-module along the fixed framing presentation. Finite presentation identifies this base change with power series in the j framing variables.
- Use delta-actions-agree to identify the first h scalar variables with the chosen diamond generators. Their augmentation is the integral coinvariant isomorphism of HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves R18.3/tw-localised-control (through delta-freeness-at-taylor-wiles-level). The remaining j variables are the framing variables, so their further augmentation gives M.

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- Q_n = ∅: M_n = M^□ and killing the framing variables gives M.
- The rank s over O[Δ\_{Q_n}][[y_{h+1},…,y_{h+j}]] does not depend on n, which the patching argument needs.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), Proposition 9.2, proof pp. 81–83 — Proof of Proposition 9.2, p. 82: the modules S_{k,ψ}(U_{Q_n}, O)\_𝔪 ⊗ R̄^{□,ψ}\_{S∪Q_n} are O[Δ\_{Q_n}][[y_{h+1},…,y_{h+j}]]-modules, free over O[Δ\_{Q_n}] of rank independent of n by Corollary 7.5, with Δ\_{Q_n}-coinvariants S_{k,ψ}(U, O)\_𝔪..

## R22.3

The arithmetic patching datum, its finite-level realization, stabilization, the patched ring and module, support, minimal finiteness and generic-fibre R = T are planned as instances of DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra R03.5 (Kisin Proposition (3.3.1): kisin-patch-datum, kisin-ring-module-patching, kisin-characteristic-zero-patching, kisin-patching-criterion) with the R03.3/R03.4/R03.6 algebra.

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.3-arithmetic-patching-data"></a>

### Patching data of level m from the Taylor–Wiles systems

**Definition.** `R22.3/arithmetic-patching-data`.

The arithmetic level-m patching datum is the ring-module datum of DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra R03.5/kisin-patch-datum at cutoff m, with Kisin's parameters specialised to S∞ = O[[y_1,…,y_{h+j}]], J = (y_1,…,y_h), B = R̄^{loc,ψ}\_S of dimension d + 1, A = B[[x_1,…,x_{h+j−d}]], R = R̄\_S^{□,ψ}, M = M^□ and s = rank_O M > 0, with c_m = (π\_O^m, (1+y_i)^{p^m}−1 for i ≤ h, y_{h+i}^{p^m} for i ≤ j) and bound r_m = s·m·p^{m(h+j)}; m_C^{(r)} is generated by r-th powers of elements of m_C, not the ideal power m_C^r. To the owner's datum S∞/c_m → D_m ↠ R/(c_mR + m_R^{(r_m)}), A ↠ D_m, m_{D_m}^{(r_m)} = 0, L_m free of rank s over S∞/c_m with L_m ↠ M^□/c_m, this target adds the arithmetic structure: D_m/JD_m ≅ R/(c_mR + m_R^{(r_m)}) as B-algebras, the framing action of y_{h+1},…,y_{h+j} on R, and the Hecke action on M^□ through 𝕋^□. Morphisms preserve these maps. The same datum underlies (D_m, L_m) in the dyadic construction, whose extra determinant data are R22.6/dyadic-finite-patching-data.

**Hypotheses.**

- O has finite residue field, B is complete Noetherian local, O-flat, a domain of dimension d + 1 with B[1/p] formally smooth (KW II §9.1.1; LocalGaloisDeformationRings R08.6/export-completed-tensor-product), and all maps are continuous local O-algebra maps. A fixed presentation and all framing choices are part of the datum.
- For dyadic data replace A by B[[x_1,…,x_{h+j+t−d}]] before taking the determinant-one quotient; m ≥ 3 in the action-chunk construction.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.2/taylor-wiles-module-system](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-taylor-wiles-module-system), `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.6/taylor-wiles-deformation-system`, `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.6/patching-numerology`, `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.5/characteristic-zero-open-quotient-patching`, [R22.2/module-system-control](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-module-system-control), `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.5/kisin-patch-datum`.

**Proof route.**

- Instantiate DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra R03.5/kisin-patch-datum with the displayed parameters; its cutoff ideal, element-power ideal and finite-free module condition are used unchanged.
- Add the arithmetic augmentation, framing and Hecke maps; reduction from level m + 1 to m is the owner's reduction together with the reduction of these maps. That the actual Taylor–Wiles system gives such data is finite-level-patching-realization.

**API and Checks.** Instantiate the coefficient ring, representation, fields, levels and actions specified above. Check the following signatures over those actual objects; no existential witness or conclusion may be added as an input merely to satisfy a Check.

- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.PatchingDatum` (structure): The displayed level-m rings, module, presentations, scalar maps and augmentation maps.
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.PatchingDatum.reduce` (functoriality): The level-(m+1) datum reduces via D/(c_m+m_D^{(r_m)}) and L/c_m, preserving every structure map.
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.PatchingDatum.isomorphism` (equivalence): An isomorphism preserves both coefficient maps, the A-presentation, the R-augmentation, the module action and the augmentation to M^□/c_m.

**Unit-test specifications.**

- `patchingDatum_rm` (computation): For s=1,p=3,m=2,h+j=1 one has r_m=18.
- `patchingDatum_power_ideal` (non-example): Over F₂[[x,y]], m^{(2)}=(x²,y²), whereas m² also contains xy.
- `patchingDatum_scalars` (compatibility): The identity of the underlying finite module is not a datum isomorphism unless it intertwines the specified S∞ and D_m actions.

**Acceptance checks.**

- For h = 0, choosing Q_n empty and fixed framings makes the chosen finite-level system constant.
- The rank s of L_m over 𝒪⟦y⟧/c_m does not depend on m or n.
- Without the bound 𝔟\_n ⊆ ((1 + y_i)^{p^n} − 1), L_m would not be free over 𝒪⟦y⟧/c_m.

**Sources.** [KISIN-FFLAT-2009](https://people.math.harvard.edu/~kisin/dvifiles/bt.dvi), Proposition (3.3.1), proof pp. 62–64, equations (3.3.2)–(3.3.3) — Finite-level truncation constants and the full datum; [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), proof of Proposition 9.3, pp. 84–86 — Augmentation isomorphism, shifted index and dyadic enhancement.

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.3-finite-level-patching-realization"></a>

### Arithmetic realization of each finite patching level

**Lemma.** `R22.3/finite-level-patching-realization`.

Fix a≥0 with p^a>N for the isotropy exponent; at p=2 also require a≥2. For all sufficiently large n≥m (m≥3 at p=2), put D_{m,n}=R_{n+a}/(c_mR_{n+a}+m_{R_{n+a}}^{(r_m)}) and L_{m,n}=M_{n+a}/c_mM_{n+a}. These are level-m data. The annihilator b_ℓ of M_ℓ satisfies b_ℓ⊂((1+y_i)^{p^{ℓ−a}}−1:i≤h), so b_{n+a}⊂c_m. If b is the number of generators of m_A, set r′\_m=b r_m; m_A^{r′\_m}⊂m_A^{(r_m)}, so every D_{m,n} is a quotient of the finite ring A/m_A^{r′\_m}. The augmentation to R/(c_m+m_R^{(r_m)}) is an isomorphism after J, and the corresponding module augmentation is equivariant.

**Hypotheses.**

- N is the isotropy exponent of KW II §7.4 and a is fixed with p^a > N (a ≥ 2 at p = 2); M_ℓ is finite free over O[[y]]/𝔟\_ℓ of rank s with 𝔟\_ℓ ⊂ ((1+y_i)^{p^{ℓ−a}} − 1)\_{i≤h} for ℓ > a (Corollary 7.5).

**Prerequisites.** [R22.3/arithmetic-patching-data](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.3-arithmetic-patching-data), [R22.2/module-system-control](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-module-system-control), `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.6/taylor-wiles-deformation-system`, `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.5/kisin-patch-datum`.

**Proof route.**

- Use Δ\_Q=Δ′\_Q/Δ′\_Q[N] and the fixed shift a, so that reduction of M_{n+a} modulo c_m is free of rank s. The unshifted level-n bound with p^n is not available for the isotropy quotient.
- A maximal-ideal element acts nilpotently on the s-dimensional residual augmentation of M_n. The nilpotence estimate in Kisin (3.3.1) gives its r_m-th power zero on L_{m,n}; thus the D_{m,n}-action is defined.
- Surjectivity of the global presentation gives A↠D_{m,n}. The pigeonhole bound on b maximal-ideal generators gives m_A^{b r_m}⊂m_A^{(r_m)}, yielding a finite ambient truncation. Quotient the known augmentation kernels and retain all scalar actions.

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- If the isotropy groups have order prime to p (Gee's case p ≥ 5 unramified), Δ\_v = Δ′\_v and 𝔟\_n ⊂ ((1+y_i)^{p^n} − 1) without a shift, which is Kisin's unshifted D_{m,n} = R_n/(c_mR_n + m^{(r_m)}); the shift by a is needed only when the isotropy groups have p-torsion.
- For b = 2 generators and r_m = 3, m_A^6 ⊂ m_A^{(3)}: a monomial of degree 6 in two generators has an exponent at least 3.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), Proposition 9.2 proof, pp. 81–83; Proposition 9.3 proof, pp. 84–86 — Kisin's proof of (3.3.1) defines D_{m,n} = R_n/(c_mR_n + m^{(r_m)}) and L_{m,n} = M_n/c_mM_n and shows a^{r_m} kills L_{m,n}; KW II's proof of Proposition 9.3, pp. 85–86, shifts to level n + a (2^a > N, a ≥ 2), uses b_n ⊂ ((1+y_i)^{2^{n−a}} − 1), and bounds m^{r′} ⊂ m^{(r)} with r′ = br for b generators..

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.3-patching-data-finite-and-compatible"></a>

### Finite stabilization with all comparison maps

**Lemma.** `R22.3/patching-data-finite-and-compatible`.

At each fixed m there are finitely many isomorphism classes of the level-m arithmetic data, including every coefficient, presentation, augmentation and module-action map. There is a diagonal subsequence n_i and compatible identifications between the stabilized level-m data and the reductions of level-(m+1) data. The ring transition is the quotient by c_m+m^{(r_m)} and the module transition is quotient by c_m. Every coefficient, deformation and Hecke comparison square commutes. At p=2 stabilize additionally the determinant map, action chunks, embeddings and the small-kernel determinant-one comparison.

**Hypotheses.**

- Each level-m datum is a finite set of finite rings, modules and maps of bounded cardinality (a quotient of A/m_A^{r′\_m} and a free S∞/c_m-module of rank s with chosen basis), so there are finitely many isomorphism classes.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.3/finite-level-patching-realization](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.3-finite-level-patching-realization), `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.5/characteristic-zero-open-quotient-patching`, `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.5/finite-decorated-data`, `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.5/patching-extraction`.

**Proof route.**

- A/m_A^{r′\_m} and S∞/c_m are finite sets, so there are finitely many ring quotients, free-module action tables and structure maps of the required cardinalities. The same argument applies to the truncated dyadic action data.
- At successive m choose infinite subsequences and identifications of the complete structured data, not just their isomorphism classes after forgetting maps. Diagonalize and choose compatible reductions.
- Apply the owner’s cofinal open-quotient patching with scalar action through the deformation ring. The ideals contain powers of the full maximal ideal; no passage using only π-power truncations is made.

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- Stabilising isomorphism classes after forgetting the Hecke or augmentation maps would not give compatible transition maps on those structures in the limit.
- At p = 2 the determinant map, the action chunk and the comparison D″\_m ↠ D_m are stabilised together with the pair (D_m, L_m).

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), Proposition 9.3 proof, pp. 86–87 — Proof of Proposition 9.3, pp. 86–87: after the finite-level data are defined, finiteness of the number of isomorphism classes of patching data of level m gives a subsequence along which the data stabilise compatibly, as in Kisin's proof of (3.3.1); the limits R∞, R′∞, M∞ are then taken..

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.3-patched-ring-and-module"></a>

### The patched ring and module

**Theorem.** `R22.3/patched-ring-and-module`.

There are surjections of B-algebras B⟦x_1, …, x_{h+j−d}⟧ ↠ R_∞ ↠ R̄^{□,ψ}\_S, with R_∞ an 𝒪⟦y_1, …, y_{h+j}⟧-algebra and R_∞/(y_1, …, y_h) ≅ R̄^{□,ψ}\_S. There is an R_∞-module M_∞, finite free over 𝒪⟦y_1, …, y_{h+j}⟧, with M_∞/(y_1, …, y_h) ≅ M^□ (R22.1/framed-hecke-module) as modules over the specialised deformation ring, with M^□ faithful over 𝕋^□\_𝔪, and with R_∞ → End(M_∞) compatible with 𝒪⟦y⟧. Moreover R_∞/(y_1, …, y_{h+j}) ≅ R̄^ψ\_S.

**Hypotheses.**

- Use the fixed presentations, positive constant rank s, scalar-compatible module actions and the full structured stabilization. The limit construction is DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra R03.5/kisin-ring-module-patching (Kisin Proposition (3.3.1)); this theorem applies it to the arithmetic data and carries the augmentation, framing and Hecke maps through the limit.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.3/arithmetic-patching-data](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.3-arithmetic-patching-data), [R22.1/framed-hecke-module](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-framed-hecke-module), `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.5/characteristic-zero-open-quotient-patching`, [R22.1/framed-hecke-properties](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-framed-hecke-properties), [R22.1/framed-deformation-to-hecke-map](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-framed-deformation-to-hecke-map), [R22.3/finite-level-patching-realization](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.3-finite-level-patching-realization), [R22.3/patching-data-finite-and-compatible](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.3-patching-data-finite-and-compatible), `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.5/kisin-ring-module-patching`.

**Proof route.**

- Apply DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra R03.5/kisin-ring-module-patching to the arithmetic data of finite-level-patching-realization, stabilised by patching-data-finite-and-compatible: R∞ = lim D_m and M∞ = lim L_m along the transition maps, with A ↠ R∞ from the A-presentations.
- Pass the augmentation identities and scalar action diagrams to the limit: R∞/J≅R̄\_S^{□,ψ}, M∞/J≅M^□. Killing the framing variables recovers the unframed ring and module. Freeness over S∞ follows from the compatible rank-s trivializations.

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- With h = 0, R_∞ = R̄^{□,ψ}\_S and M_∞ = M^□.
- M_∞/(y_1, …, y_{h+j}) ≅ S_{k,ψ}(U, 𝒪)\_𝔪.
- Integral and inverted-π assertions are kept separate: M_∞ is free over 𝒪⟦y⟧ integrally.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), Proposition 9.2 (I), p. 81 — Proposition 9.2 (I), p. 81: maps R̄\_{S,loc,ψ}[[x_1,…,x_{h+j−d}]] → R∞ → R̄\_{S,ψ}, with R∞ an O[[y_1,…,y_{h+j}]]-algebra, R∞/(y_1,…,y_h) ≅ R̄\_{S,ψ}, and M∞ finite free over O[[y]] whose quotient by (y_1,…,y_h) is a faithful 𝕋^□-module.; [GEE-MLT-2022](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2202.05818v2), §5.6, p. 40 — §5.6, p. 40: the patching set-up, with ideals c_N of J∞ such that S_{Q_N}/c_N is finite free over J∞/c_N and open ideals d_N of R^univ_∅ inside the kernel to End(S_∅/λ^N), from which R∞ and S∞ are formed along a subsequence..

**Planet.** Patched ring and module.

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.3-patched-support"></a>

### Support of the patched module

**Theorem.** `R22.3/patched-support`.

dim R_∞ ≤ 1 + h + j and depth_{R_∞}(M_∞) ≥ 1 + h + j, so every minimal prime of Supp_{R_∞}(M_∞) is a minimal prime of R_∞ of maximal dimension. Supp M_∞ is a union of irreducible components of Spec R_∞. If B is a domain (KW II: semistable conditions at the finite places of S not above p), then B⟦x⟧ → R_∞ → End(M_∞) is injective, R_∞ ≅ B⟦x_1, …, x_{h+j−d}⟧, and Supp M_∞ = Spec R_∞.

**Hypotheses.**

- R∞ is complete Noetherian local; M∞≠0 is finite over R∞ and finite free of positive rank over the regular local S∞. The S∞ scalar action factors through R∞. The image of R∞ in End_{S∞}(M∞) is finite over S∞.
- Use the Global R04.6 dimension upper bound dim R∞≤1+h+j. For the stronger isomorphism assume B is an O-flat domain of dimension d+1, so A=B[[x]] is a domain of dimension 1+h+j.
- Use DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra R03.5/kisin-patching-criterion (regular-base depth and dimension), the R03.3 finite-map depth comparison for the action image and the R03.6 maximal-CM component-support theorem. No full-support claim is inferred from maximal depth alone when A is reducible.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.3/patched-ring-and-module](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.3-patched-ring-and-module), `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.6/patching-numerology`, `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.6/nearly-faithful-and-component-support-conclusions`, `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.3/finite-map-cm-transfer`, `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.3/cohen-macaulay-and-full-support`, `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.3/cm-completion-and-equidimensionality`, `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.5/kisin-patching-criterion`.

**Proof route.**

- Pass to the finite action image to compare depth with S∞; this yields depth_{R∞}M∞≥1+h+j. The dimension inequality makes M∞ maximal Cohen–Macaulay, so its support is a union of top-dimensional components.
- In the domain case the action image has dimension 1+h+j because it is finite over, and contains faithfully, S∞. A nonzero kernel in A would lower the dimension. Thus A→R∞→End(M∞) is injective and A≅R∞ with faithful module.
- In the reducible case retain only the union-of-components conclusion until the separate component-matching or Ihara-avoidance target proves every component occurs.

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- Gee's setting: dim R_∞ = dim J_∞ = 4#T + r, and R′\_∞ has a unique minimal prime, so Supp S′\_∞ = Spec R′\_∞.
- If B has several components (for example with an inertia-rigid place), the support is only known to be a union of components; component matching is R22.4.
- Maximal depth alone does not give full support: R03.6's example k⟦x, y⟧/(xy) with M = A/(x).

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), proof of Proposition 9.2 (II), p. 83 — Proof of Proposition 9.2 (II), p. 83: the image of R̄\_{S,loc,ψ}[[x]] in End(M∞) is finite and faithful over O[[y]], hence of dimension ≥ h + j + 1; since R̄\_{S,loc,ψ} is a domain and R̄\_{S,loc,ψ}[[x]] has dimension h + j + 1, the composite to End(M∞) is injective and R∞ ≅ R̄\_{S,loc,ψ}[[x]].; [GEE-MLT-2022](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2202.05818v2), §5.6, pp. 40–41 — §5.6, pp. 40–41: depth_{J∞}(S∞) = 4#T + r gives depth_{R∞}(S∞) ≥ 4#T + r = dim R∞, so minimal primes of the support are minimal primes of R∞, and R′∞, having a unique minimal prime, is fully supported..

**Planet.** Support of the patched module.

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.3-minimal-ring-finite"></a>

### Finiteness of the minimal deformation ring

**Theorem.** `R22.3/minimal-ring-finite`.

In the situation of patched-support with B a domain, R̄^{□,ψ}\_S is finite over 𝒪⟦y_{h+1}, …, y_{h+j}⟧ and R̄^ψ\_S is finite over 𝒪.

**Hypotheses.**

- This is finiteness at the minimal level of a modular ρ̄, as a consequence of patching. The global finiteness used for arbitrary ρ̄ (KW II Theorem 10.1, by base change) is PotentialModularityAndCompatibleSystems R24.1, not this node.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.3/patched-support](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.3-patched-support), `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.4/finite-of-faithful-finite-module`.

**Proof route.**

- R_∞ ≅ B⟦x⟧ acts faithfully on M_∞, which is finite over 𝒪⟦y⟧, so R_∞ is finite over 𝒪⟦y⟧ (DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra R03.4/finite-of-faithful-finite-module).
- Killing y_1, …, y_h gives R̄^{□,ψ}\_S finite over 𝒪⟦y_{h+1}, …, y_{h+j}⟧; killing the framing variables gives R̄^ψ\_S finite over 𝒪.

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- R̄^{□,ψ}\_S itself is never finite over 𝒪 (it has j = 4|S| − 1 framing variables).
- With finiteness and dim ≥ 1 (GlobalGaloisDeformations R04.3), R̄^ψ\_S has characteristic-zero points.
- The statement is for the minimal-level ring of a modular ρ̄ only.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), Proposition 9.2 (II), p. 81 — Proposition 9.2 (II), p. 81: R̄^□\_{S,ψ} is a finite O[[y_{h+1},…,y_{h+j}]]-module and R̄^ψ\_S is a finite O-module..

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.3-generic-fibre-r-equals-t"></a>

### R = T after inverting p

**Theorem.** `R22.3/generic-fibre-r-equals-t`.

If B is a domain, the surjection π^□ : R̄^{□,ψ}\_S → 𝕋^□\_𝔪 of R22.1 has p-power torsion kernel. Equivalently, R̄^{□,ψ}\_S[1/p] ≅ 𝕋^□\_𝔪[1/p], and likewise R̄^ψ\_S[1/p] ≅ 𝕋\_ψ(U)\_𝔪[1/p].

**Hypotheses.**

- B is O-flat, a domain and has regular generic fibre; R∞≅B[[x]] is Noetherian and its generic fibre is regular. M∞ is nonzero, finite over R∞, free of positive rank over S∞=O[[y_1,…,y_{h+j}]], with the S∞ action factoring through R∞.
- Use the finite action image, localize at each prime and apply the owner’s finite-map depth comparison and Auslander–Buchsbaum. The dimension equality and regular scalar parameters are part of the patching numerology; regularity of the ring alone does not make every finite module projective.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.3/patched-support](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.3-patched-support), [R22.1/deformation-to-hecke-surjective](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-deformation-to-hecke-surjective), `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.6/faithful-quotient-ring-identification`, `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.6/maximal-flat-quotient`, `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.3/depth-auslander-buchsbaum-and-dimension-bounds`, `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.3/finite-map-cm-transfer`, `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.3/regularity-localization-and-descent`, `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.5/kisin-characteristic-zero-patching`, `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.6/torsion-in-kernel`.

**Proof route.**

- R∞≅B[[x]] by the domain dimension argument. The local deformation supplier gives regularity of R∞[1/p].
- M∞[1/p] is finite projective and faithful over R∞[1/p]: this is the conclusion of DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra R03.5/kisin-characteristic-zero-patching for the arithmetic data, whose proof uses the R03.3 finite-map depth comparison and Auslander–Buchsbaum at each point; its local rank is positive by patched-support.
- The augmentation R∞ → R̄\_S^{□,ψ} is the quotient by J, and base change preserves a finite projective module of positive rank, hence faithfulness after inverting p. Its action factors through the framed Hecke image, so the kernel of R̄\_S^{□,ψ} → 𝕋^□ is p-power torsion (DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra R03.6/torsion-in-kernel); finite generation gives a single power, and unframing gives the unframed comparison.

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- Since T is O-flat and reduced, the comparison identifies the maximal O-flat quotient of R̄\_S^ψ with T (R03.6/maximal-flat-quotient and faithful-quotient-ring-identification).
- The integral isomorphism needs more: see integral-r-equals-t-when-smooth (R22.4).
- If B is not a domain, one gets only a component-by-component statement (R22.4).

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), Proposition 9.2 (III) and its proof, pp. 81 and 83 — Proposition 9.2 (III), p. 81, with proof on p. 83: π : R̄^□\_{S,ψ} → 𝕋^□ is surjective with p-power torsion kernel; the proof applies Kisin's Lemma (3.3.4) to O[[y]][1/p] → R∞[1/p] to make M∞ ⊗ E finite projective and faithful..

**Planet.** Generic-fibre R = T.

## R22.4

Avoidance data and its special-fibre comparison are separate. Component support and reducedness are preserved explicitly; the integral refinement uses the global numerical relation bound, not finiteness alone.

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.4-ihara-avoidance-comparison"></a>

### Two deformation problems that agree modulo λ

**Construction.** `R22.4/ihara-avoidance-comparison`.

From the specified residual/global problem, construct the two arithmetic avoidance problems with the same primes T_r away from p, trivial residual local representations and q_v≡1 mod p. At v∈T_r impose respectively inertia characteristic polynomials (X−1)² and (X−ζ)(X−ζ⁻¹), where ζ is a nontrivial p-th root of unity. Use the local owner’s two rings and the automorphic owner’s corresponding coefficient characters/levels, with the same framings and global determinant. Record the finite-level comparison data to be stabilized. The special-fibre isomorphisms and compatible patched comparisons are proved in avoidance-special-fibre-comparison, separately from constructing the pair.

**Hypotheses.**

- The local geometry used is LocalGaloisDeformationRings R08.2/ihara-avoidance-components (Taylor II Proposition 3.1): (R^{loc,′})^red is irreducible, O-flat of Krull dimension 1 + 3#T + [F : ℚ], and the components of R^loc biject with those of R^loc/λ.
- This is Taylor's Ihara avoidance, which replaces Ihara's lemma in passing to nonminimal levels.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.3/patched-ring-and-module](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.3-patched-ring-and-module), `LocalGaloisDeformationRings:R08.2/ihara-avoidance-components`.

**Proof route.**

- Use Local R08.2’s avoidance rings with their specified residual identifications and the compatible global local-condition quotients.
- Choose corresponding coefficient lattices/levels and the same auxiliary primes and framings for the two problems. Include their comparison maps in the finite structured data.
- The special-fibre comparison theorem verifies that these maps induce the required equivariant isomorphisms; local generic component geometry is imported from R08.2/ihara-avoidance-components.

**API and Checks.** Instantiate the coefficient ring, representation, fields, levels and actions specified above. Check the following signatures over those actual objects; no existential witness or conclusion may be added as an input merely to satisfy a Check.

- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.avoidancePair` (constructor): The problems 𝒮\_Q and 𝒮′\_Q with their local rings and levels.
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.avoidancePair_mod_lambda` (compatibility): R^loc/λ = R^{loc,′}/λ and S/λ = S′/λ. Proved by [R22.4/avoidance-special-fibre-comparison](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.4-avoidance-special-fibre-comparison).
- `TauCeti.ModularityLifting.avoidancePair_irreducible` (characterisation): (R^{loc,′})^red is irreducible.

**Unit-test specifications.**

- `avoidancePair_Tr_empty` (degenerate): T_r = ∅ gives equal problems.
- `avoidancePair_mod_lambda` (characterisation): The local conditions agree modulo λ.
- `avoidancePair_types_differ` (non-example): The generic fibres carry different inertial types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- T_r = ∅: the two problems coincide.
- Each ring's generic fibre sees a different inertial type (unipotent versus (ζ, ζ^{−1})), although the special fibres coincide.
- Without #k(v) ≡ 1 mod p there is no nontrivial ζ-type at v.

**Sources.** [GEE-MLT-2022](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2202.05818v2), §5.6, pp. 33–35 — §5.6, pp. 33–35: the paired problems S_Q and S′\_Q, with char ρ(σ\_v) = (X − 1)² resp. (X − ζ)(X − ζ^{−1}) at v ∈ T_r, the irreducibility of (R^{loc,′})^{red}, and the identification of the two problems modulo λ..

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.4-integral-r-equals-t-when-smooth"></a>

### When the R = T statement is integral

**Theorem.** `R22.4/integral-r-equals-t-when-smooth`.

Suppose B = R̄^{□,loc,ψ}\_S is formally smooth over 𝒪, as when every local condition is formally smooth (Fontaine–Laffaille, ordinary with ρ̄\_v ramified, odd archimedean for p ≠ 2). Then R_∞ ≅ B⟦x⟧ is a power series ring over 𝒪, M_∞ is free over R_∞, R̄^ψ\_S ≅ 𝕋\_ψ(U)\_𝔪 integrally, S_{k,ψ}(U, 𝒪)\_𝔪 is free over 𝕋\_ψ(U)\_𝔪, and R̄^ψ\_S is a complete intersection. More generally, in the KW II Proposition 4.5/Lemma 4.6 presentation with its numerical relation bound and dimension comparison, if each R̄^{□,ψ}\_v is Cohen–Macaulay (resp. Gorenstein, resp. a complete intersection), so is R̄^ψ\_S provided it is finite over 𝒪. The presentation bound, not finiteness alone, makes the displayed relations and framing variables part of a system of parameters.

**Hypotheses.**

- Maximal depth over a regular local ring gives freeness (DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra R03.3/free-of-maximal-depth-regular-local); integral R = T from a free module is R03.6/r-equals-t-of-free and patched-module-r-equals-t.
- The Cohen–Macaulay/Gorenstein/complete-intersection clause is in the KW II global deformation presentation with its generator–relation dimension bound, not an arbitrary finite quotient of B⟦x⟧.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.3/patched-support](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.3-patched-support), `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.3/depth-auslander-buchsbaum-and-dimension-bounds`, `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.6/faithful-quotient-ring-identification`, `LocalGaloisDeformationRings:R08.6`, `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.3/local-to-global-presentation`, `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.3/global-dimension-lower-bound`, `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.3/dimension-count-ci-flatness`, `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.3/flat-depth-dimension-formulas`, `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.3/gorenstein-local-rings`, `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.3/canonical-type-and-base-change`.

**Proof route.**

- R_∞ ≅ B⟦x⟧ is regular, and M_∞ has maximal depth, so M_∞ is free over R_∞ (R03.3).
- M_∞ is free over R_∞, and y_1, …, y_{h+j} is regular on it, so S(U)\_𝔪 = M_∞/(y) is free over R_∞/(y) = R̄^ψ\_S. Hence R̄^ψ\_S → 𝕋 is an isomorphism (R03.6/r-equals-t-of-free).
- For the additional CM/Gorenstein/CI clause, use the Global R04.3 fixed-determinant presentation and the explicit KW II Proposition 4.5/Lemma 4.6 bound. Let g be the number of added variables, r the number of displayed relations and j the framing dimension. Finiteness over O gives dim R̄^□=j+1. The dimension lower bound and the relation bound force dim(B[[x]])=j+1+r. The relations together with j framing parameters and π form a system of parameters. When B is CM they are a regular sequence; the owner’s regular-quotient and type/base-change facts transfer CM, Gorenstein and CI as applicable. Finiteness without that equality is insufficient.
- Complete intersection: a regular ring modulo a regular sequence.

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- Wiles and Taylor–Wiles minimal case: R ≅ 𝕋 is a complete intersection.
- With a non-smooth local factor (for example Savitt's 𝒪⟦T₁, …, T₅⟧/(T₁T₂ − p)), only the generic-fibre statement generic-fibre-r-equals-t is obtained without further input.
- Faithfulness of S(U)\_𝔪 over R̄ is exactly equivalent to integral R = T (R03.6/r-equals-t-free); freeness is stronger.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), §4.2, remarks after Lemma 4.6, p. 45 — Remarks after Lemma 4.6, p. 45: with r_1,…,r_{r(J)} lifting a basis of J/𝔪J and R̄^ψ\_S finite over O, y_1,…,y_{4|S|−1}, r_1,…,r_{r(J)}, p is a system of parameters of R̄\_{S,loc,ψ}[[X]]; when the R̄\_{v,ψ} are Cohen–Macaulay it is a regular sequence, so R̄^ψ\_S is O-flat and Cohen–Macaulay (Gorenstein, complete intersection) when the R̄\_{v,ψ} are..

**Planet.** Integral R = T.

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.4-avoidance-special-fibre-comparison"></a>

### Special-fibre comparison for the avoidance pair

**Lemma.** `R22.4/avoidance-special-fibre-comparison`.

For the avoidance problems with ζ≡1 mod λ, their local deformation conditions and coefficient characters reduce to the same data. Using the same auxiliary primes, framings, coefficient lattices and branch labels gives identifications of the two global rings and Hecke modules modulo λ, equivariant for the common scalar action. Stabilize these comparison maps together with the two patching systems, so R_∞/λ≅R′\_∞/λ and M_∞/λ≅M′\_∞/λ with the common local-ring action.

**Hypotheses.**

- At v ∈ T_r, ρ̄|G_{F_v} is trivial and #k(v) ≡ 1 mod p; ζ is a fixed primitive p-th root of unity in L, so ζ ≡ 1 mod λ. The same auxiliary primes Q, framings and coefficient choices are used for both problems.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.4/ihara-avoidance-comparison](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.4-ihara-avoidance-comparison), [R22.3/patched-ring-and-module](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.3-patched-ring-and-module), `LocalGaloisDeformationRings:R08.2/ihara-avoidance-components`, `LocalGaloisDeformationRings:R08.2/ihara-avoidance-rings-p2`.

**Proof route.**

- The chosen root ζ reduces to one; the specified local quotients therefore have the same special fibre by the local avoidance theorem. The chosen coefficient characters and their lattices also reduce compatibly.
- Apply the global quotient universal property and the equivariant finite-level coefficient comparison with identical primes and labels. Add these maps and their commuting squares to the finite structured isomorphism classes before passing to a common subsequence.
- The compatible inverse limit and finite-module quotient exactness identify the two patched special fibres and modules with their common scalar action. Patching unrelated subsequences would not provide these comparisons.

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- T_r = ∅: the two problems coincide and the comparison is the identity.
- If the two patching systems were stabilised along unrelated subsequences, the mod-λ identification of R∞ and R′∞ would not follow.

**Sources.** [GEE-MLT-2022](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2202.05818v2), §5.6, pp. 33–35 and 40–41 — §5.6, pp. 33–35: Gee defines the paired problems S_Q and S′\_Q with char ρ(σ\_v) = (X − 1)² resp. (X − ζ)(X − ζ⁻¹) at v ∈ T_r, notes R^loc/λ = R^{loc,′}/λ because ζ ≡ 1 mod λ, and fixes universal deformations compatible modulo λ; pp. 40–41 patch the two systems together so that the patched objects agree modulo λ..

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.4-support-transfer-mod-lambda"></a>

### Transfer of full support across the special fibre

**Theorem.** `R22.4/support-transfer-mod-lambda`.

In the setting of ihara-avoidance-comparison, Supp_{R′\_∞}(S′\_∞) = Spec R′\_∞. Hence Supp_{R_∞/λ}(S_∞/λ) = Spec R_∞/λ, and since the irreducible components of Spec R_∞ biject with those of Spec R_∞/λ, Supp_{R_∞}(S_∞) = Spec R_∞. Specialising along 𝔞\_∞, Supp_{R^univ_∅}(S_∅) = Spec R^univ_∅.

**Hypotheses.**

- The components of Spec R_∞ versus Spec R_∞/λ come from R^loc (R08.2/ihara-avoidance-components, Gee Theorem 3.38). The lift of near faithfulness from the special fibre and its descent along the patching ideal are DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra R03.6.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.4/ihara-avoidance-comparison](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.4-ihara-avoidance-comparison), [R22.3/patched-support](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.3-patched-support), `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.6/nearly-faithful-and-component-support-conclusions`, [R22.4/avoidance-special-fibre-comparison](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.4-avoidance-special-fibre-comparison), `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.6/completion-generic-special-components`.

**Proof route.**

- R′\_∞ has a unique minimal prime and S′\_∞ has maximal depth, so its support is all of Spec R′\_∞ (patched-support, R03.6/maximal-cm-nearly-faithful-irreducible).
- Reduce mod λ: S′\_∞/λ = S_∞/λ and R′\_∞/λ = R_∞/λ, so S_∞/λ has full support.
- Supp S_∞ is a union of components (maximal depth) containing all of Spec R_∞/λ, and the components biject, so Supp S_∞ = Spec R_∞ (R03.6/nearly-faithful-lift-from-special-fibre).
- Descend along 𝔞\_∞ = (𝔞, y_1, …, y_r) (R03.6/patching-nearly-faithful-descends).

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- T_r = ∅: the primed and unprimed problems agree, and the argument is patched-support.
- The unprimed R^loc may have several components (unipotent types); the primed one is irreducible, which is the point of the comparison.
- The conclusion is support, that is near faithfulness, not an integral R = T.

**Sources.** [GEE-MLT-2022](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2202.05818v2), §5.6, p. 41 — §5.6, p. 41: Supp S′∞ = Spec R′∞, then reduction modulo λ and the bijection between components of Spec R∞ and Spec R∞/λ give Supp S∞ = Spec R∞, which descends to Supp_{R^univ_∅}(S_∅) = Spec R^univ_∅..

**Planet.** Ihara avoidance.

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.4-modularity-from-full-support"></a>

### Modularity from full support

**Theorem.** `R22.4/modularity-from-full-support`.

If Supp_{R^univ_∅}(S_∅) = Spec R^univ_∅, then ker(R^univ_∅ → 𝕋\_∅) is nilpotent, (R^univ_∅)^red ≅ 𝕋\_∅, and every lift ρ of type 𝒮\_∅ over 𝒪 is modular: it corresponds to a homomorphism 𝕋\_∅ → 𝒪, hence to a cuspidal π of weight (k, η) with ρ ≅ ρ\_{π,ı}.

**Hypotheses.**

- 𝕋\_∅ acts faithfully on S_∅ by definition; the ring-theoretic step is DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra R03.6 (r-to-t-kernel-nil, r-red-equals-t-red).

**Prerequisites.** [R22.4/support-transfer-mod-lambda](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.4-support-transfer-mod-lambda), `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.6/faithful-quotient-ring-identification`, `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:R18.6`, `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.6/nearly-faithful-iff-support-eq-univ`.

**Proof route.**

- Full support means S_∅ is nearly faithful over R^univ_∅ (DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra R03.6/nearly-faithful-iff-support-eq-univ), so the kernel of R^univ_∅ → 𝕋\_∅ ⊆ End(S_∅) is nil (R03.6/r-to-t-kernel-nil).
- An 𝒪-point of R^univ_∅ factors through the reduced quotient, which is 𝕋\_∅, and 𝒪-points of 𝕋\_∅ are eigenforms (R18.3 via R18.6, Jacquet–Langlands).

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- This is Gee Lemma 5.7.
- Only the reduced quotient is identified: nilpotents in R^univ_∅ are not excluded.
- The conclusion is modularity of points of the given type, not of arbitrary lifts.

**Sources.** [GEE-MLT-2022](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2202.05818v2), Lemma 5.7, p. 35 — Lemma 5.7, p. 35: if S_∅ has full support over R^univ_∅ then the kernel of R^univ_∅ → 𝕋\_∅ is nilpotent, (R^univ_∅)^{red} ≅ 𝕋\_∅, and the point of ρ gives an eigenform π with ρ ≅ ρ\_{π,ı}..

**Planet.** Modularity from full support.

## R22.5

Odd-prime KW, Kisin, Fontaine–Laffaille and BCDT type/extended-type targets are planned with distinct hypotheses. BCDT uses the published CDT corrections. The nonordinary endpoint uses the read Durham Q theorem; the ordinary endpoint retains its exact supplier request.

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.5-solvable-base-change-reduction"></a>

### Reduction to a solvable totally real extension

**Lemma.** `R22.5/solvable-base-change-reduction`.

Let ρ : G_F → GL_2(𝒪) be continuous with F totally real, S a finite set of places of F, and L_v/F_v finite Galois for v ∈ S (real L_v at the infinite places). There is a finite solvable Galois totally real extension F′/F with F′\_w ≅ L_v for every w | v ∈ S; it can be chosen linearly disjoint from any given finite extension, so that ρ̄|G_{F′} keeps a non-solvable image, and ρ̄|G_{F′(ζ\_p)} stays absolutely irreducible when ρ̄|G_{F(ζ\_p)} is. Then ρ is modular if and only if ρ|G_{F′} is modular. In particular one may assume: [F′ : ℚ] even, ρ|G_{F′} semistable with unipotent inertia at every place away from p where it ramifies, ρ̄|G_{F′} unramified away from p, and the prescribed splitting at p.

**Hypotheses.**

- Existence of the field is not an automorphic statement: it is Clozel–Harris–Taylor's Lemma 4.1.2 (R22.1/cht-soluble-prescribed-completions; Gee quotes it as Fact 4.27), and in the allowable case R22.1/allowable-base-change-existence. The descent of modularity along soluble extensions (Gee Proposition 4.25, from Langlands' cyclic base change and strong multiplicity one) is requested from GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer R17.4.
- KW II call such extensions allowable base changes; Kisin calls them admissible extensions.
- For the modularity equivalence impose that ρ|G_{F′} is irreducible, as in Gee Proposition 4.25 (automatic under the retained residual-image hypotheses used here). Local prescription and preservation of the residual image require separate finite splitting conditions.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.1/minimal-level-data](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-minimal-level-data), [R22.1/allowable-base-change-existence](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-allowable-base-change-existence), [R22.1/cht-soluble-prescribed-completions](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-cht-soluble-prescribed-completions), `GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer:R17.4`.

**Proof route.**

- Existence: Clozel–Harris–Taylor's Lemma 4.1.2 with the real places of F in the prescribed set (E_v = ℝ) gives a totally real soluble Galois F′/F with F′\_w ≅ L_v, linearly disjoint from a given finite Galois extension; taking that extension to contain the field cut out by ρ̄ and μ\_p keeps the residual image and cyclotomic irreducibility, as in R22.1/allowable-base-change-existence, which adds evenness and the conditions at p.
- Modularity descends: for a cyclic step E/F, if ρ|G_E ≅ ρ\_π then π ∘ σ ≅ π by strong multiplicity one, so π is a base change from F, and ρ ≅ ρ\_{π′} ⊗ χ for a character χ by Schur's lemma; induct along the solvable tower.
- Unipotent inertia: by Grothendieck's monodromy theorem ρ|I_v acts unipotently on an open subgroup of I_v, which a finite L_v cuts out.

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- F = ℚ, L_3 = ℚ\_3(√−3): F′ = ℚ(√6) works, since 6/(−3) = −2 is a square in ℚ\_3; ℚ(√−3) itself is not allowed, as F′ must be totally real.
- Modularity of ρ|G_{F′} descends to ρ only because F′/F is solvable: for a non-solvable F′/F descent is not available.
- Without linear disjointness the image hypothesis can fail over F′: if ρ̄ is induced from a real quadratic K/F with K ≠ F(√p\*), then ρ̄|G_{F(ζ\_p)} can be irreducible while ρ̄|G_{F′} is reducible for F′ ⊇ K.

**Sources.** [GEE-MLT-2022](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2202.05818v2), Proposition 4.25 and Fact 4.27, p. 29 — Descent of modularity along a solvable extension; Taylor's Lemma 2.2 follows it..

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.5-kw-odd-prime-lifting"></a>

### The Khare–Wintenberger lifting theorem for odd p

**Theorem.** `R22.5/kw-odd-prime-lifting`.

Let p > 2, F totally real and unramified at p, split at p if ρ̄|\_{D_p} is irreducible or k(ρ̄) = p + 1, with ρ̄\_F = ρ̄|G_F such that ρ̄|G_{F(μ\_p)} is absolutely irreducible and ρ̄\_F satisfies (α) and (β). Let ρ\_F : G_F → GL_2(𝒪) lift ρ̄\_F, be ramified at finitely many places, totally odd, and at every place above p of one of the types (A) crystalline of weight k with 2 ≤ k ≤ p + 1 (when k = p + 1, with k(ρ̄) = p + 1), (B) of weight 2 and crystalline over ℚ\_p^{nr}(μ\_p), or (C) semistable non-crystalline of weight 2 of the form (γ\_vχ\_p ∗; 0 γ\_v) with γ\_v unramified. Then ρ\_F is modular. This formulation uses no finiteness of deformation rings from KW II §10 and no potential modularity.

**Hypotheses.**

- F is totally real and ρ̄ : G_F → GL_2(𝔽) is as in KW II §8, with lifting data and determinant character ψ (GlobalGaloisDeformations R04.6/kw-deformation-data; the local conditions are LocalGaloisDeformationRings R08.6/kw-local-conditions).
- The residual input is (α) and (β) of kw-residual-modularity, not 'ρ̄ is modular'. KW I Theorem 4.1(2), stated with 'ρ̄ modular', is the output R22.5/kw-i-theorem-4-1-odd-prime of this layer, derived from this theorem, the weight part of Serre's conjecture and the cited cases as in KW II §10.2.
- Case (B) of weight two is only the part of the potentially Barsotti–Tate case that becomes semistable over ℚ\_p(μ\_p) (KW II §10.2, remark). The general potentially Barsotti–Tate case is kisin-potentially-bt-lifting.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.5/kw-residual-modularity](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.5-kw-residual-modularity), [R22.5/solvable-base-change-reduction](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.5-solvable-base-change-reduction), [R22.3/generic-fibre-r-equals-t](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.3-generic-fibre-r-equals-t), [R22.3/patched-support](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.3-patched-support), [R22.1/minimal-level-data](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.1-minimal-level-data), `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.6/kw-deformation-data`, `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.5/image-hypotheses`, `LocalGaloisDeformationRings:R08.6/kw-local-conditions`, `LocalGaloisDeformationRings:R08.6/export-completed-tensor-product`, [R22.5/kw-residual-modularity-beta](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.5-kw-residual-modularity-beta), `SerreWeightAndLevelOptimisation:R20.6/prescribed-local-type-lifts`.

**Proof route.**

- After a solvable base change (solvable-base-change-reduction; Taylor's Lemma 2.2), ρ\_F is totally odd, uniform above p of type (A), (B) or (C), of the form (γ\_vχ\_p ∗; 0 γ\_v) at the ramified places away from p, and ρ̄ is trivial above p when ρ̄ is unramified at p. Then ρ\_F and det ρ\_F prescribe lifting data and ψ, so ρ\_F is an 𝒪-point of R̄^{□,ψ}\_S.
- After a further base change, SerreWeightAndLevelOptimisation R20.6/prescribed-local-type-lifts (KW II Theorem 8.4) gives a cuspidal π′ of parallel weight fitting the same lifting data and ψ.
- R̄^{□,loc,ψ}\_S is a domain (semistable conditions away from p; LocalGaloisDeformationRings R08.6/export-completed-tensor-product), so R22.3/generic-fibre-r-equals-t gives R̄^{□,ψ}\_S[1/p] ≅ 𝕋^□\_𝔪[1/p]; the point ρ\_F factors through 𝕋 and is modular.
- Solvable base change descends modularity to F.

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- F = ℚ, p = 3, ρ = the 3-adic Tate module of 11a1: crystalline of weight 2 (case (A)), the mod-3 image is GL_2(𝔽\_3), so ρ̄|G_{ℚ(ζ\_3)} (image SL_2(𝔽\_3)) is absolutely irreducible, and (α), (β) hold with 11a1's own form; the theorem recovers its modularity.
- Weight p + 2 is outside (A): the theorem says nothing about crystalline lifts of weight > p + 1.
- If ρ̄|G_{F(μ\_p)} is reducible (for example ρ̄ induced from ℚ(√−3) at p = 3), Taylor–Wiles primes are unavailable (GlobalGaloisDeformations R04.5/image-hypotheses) and the theorem does not apply.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), §9.2, Theorem 9.7 and its proof, pp. 89–90 — Theorem 9.7 and its proof, pp. 89–90: for p > 2, F totally real unramified at p (split when ρ̄|D_p is irreducible or k(ρ̄) = p + 1), ρ̄|F(μ\_p) absolutely irreducible and (α), (β), a totally odd finitely ramified lift of type (A), (B) or (C) above p is modular; the proof reduces by allowable base change to uniform types and applies Proposition 9.2.; [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), §10.2, p. 92 — What KW II's own argument covers, and what it leaves to Kisin..

**Planet.** Khare–Wintenberger lifting theorem for odd p.

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.5-alpha-beta-from-modularity-over-q"></a>

### From 'ρ̄ modular' over ℚ to the residual hypotheses (α) and (β)

**Lemma.** `R22.5/alpha-beta-from-modularity-over-q`.

Let ρ̄ : G_ℚ → GL₂(𝔽) be of S-type and modular, normalised by a twist so that 2 ≤ k(ρ̄) ≤ p + 1 when p > 2. (1) For p > 2, ρ̄ arises from S_{k(ρ̄)}(Γ₁(N)) for some N prime to p, and from S₂(Γ₁(Np)). For p = 2, ρ̄ arises from S₂(Γ₁(N)) with N odd when k(ρ̄) = 2, and from a weight-two form whose level has 2-adic valuation at most 1 in both cases k(ρ̄) = 2, 4. (2) Let F be a totally real field with a soluble tower over ℚ, unramified at p, with ρ̄|\_{G_F} absolutely irreducible. Then ρ̄|\_{G_F} satisfies (α) and (β) of KW II §8.2 if p > 2; if p = 2 it satisfies (β), and (α) when k(ρ̄) = 2.

**Hypotheses.**

- 'Modular' is in the sense of KW I §1: ρ̄ arises from a newform of some weight k ≥ 2 and some level.
- The optimal prime-to-p level N(ρ̄) is not needed, only some level prime to p; this is why Gross's hypothesis N > 4 is harmless (KW II §10.2).
- This is the weight part of Serre's conjecture, imported from SerreWeightAndLevelOptimisation: Edixhoven's theorem (weight k(ρ̄) at a level prime to p, from Gross's Theorem 13.10 and Coleman–Voloch), removal of the p-part of the level, and the passage to weight 2 at level Np (Gross's Propositions 8.13 and 8.18; Ribet). The supplier nodes for p = 2 and p = 3 have restrictions (ℓ ≥ 3 for the level step, ℓ > 3 or N > 3 for weight two); the cases they leave are requested from R20.6.
- No potential modularity (KW II Theorem 6.1) and no finiteness of deformation rings (Theorem 10.1) enter.
- Edixhoven's theorem produces a Katz form in characteristic p, while (α) and (β) ask for automorphic representations; the lift to characteristic zero in weight ≥ 2 is a step of the proof, not part of the weight theorem.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.5/kw-residual-modularity](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.5-kw-residual-modularity), [R22.5/kw-residual-modularity-beta](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.5-kw-residual-modularity-beta), `SerreWeightAndLevelOptimisation:R20.3/edixhoven-weight-theorem`, `SerreWeightAndLevelOptimisation:R20.4/strip-ell-power-from-level`, `SerreWeightAndLevelOptimisation:R20.4/weight-two-at-level-n-ell`, `SerreWeightAndLevelOptimisation:R20.6`, `AlgebraicModularFormsAndSerreWeights:R15.6/s-type-arises-from-and-modular`, `AlgebraicModularFormsAndSerreWeights:R15.2/integral-lattice-and-reduction-image`, `AlgebraicModularFormsAndSerreWeights:R15.5/reduction-to-weight-at-least-two-and-to-a-true-eigenform`, `GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer:R17.4`.

**Proof route.**

- Level prime to p: ρ̄ arises from Γ₁(Np^a) for some a ≥ 0, hence from Γ₁(N) in some weight (SerreWeightAndLevelOptimisation R20.4/strip-ell-power-from-level, for p ≥ 3; R20.6 for p = 2).
- Weight k(ρ̄) at level N: Edixhoven's theorem (R20.3/edixhoven-weight-theorem), with the normalisation 2 ≤ k(ρ̄) ≤ p + 1, gives a Katz eigenform over 𝔽̄\_p of type (N, k(ρ̄), ε). Lift it to characteristic zero: after enlarging N to a multiple N ≥ 5 prime to p, the reduction map on cusp forms of weight k(ρ̄) ≥ 2 is onto (AlgebraicModularFormsAndSerreWeights R15.2/integral-lattice-and-reduction-image; the cusp-sheaf H¹ has no torsion in weight ≥ 2), and the Deligne–Serre lemma gives a characteristic-zero eigenform with the same eigenvalues modulo p, hence a newform of weight k(ρ̄) and level dividing N from which ρ̄ arises (R15.5/reduction-to-weight-at-least-two-and-to-a-true-eigenform). This is where the hypothesis N > 4 of KW II §10.2 enters.
- Weight 2 at level Np: R20.4/weight-two-at-level-n-ell (p > 3 or N > 3; enlarge N by an auxiliary prime if N ≤ 3). The p-component of the resulting newform has conductor exponent at most 1.
- Over F: take the cuspidal automorphic representations of GL₂(𝔸\_ℚ) of these newforms and base-change them along the soluble tower (GL2AutomorphicRepresentationsAndTransfer R17.4); they stay cuspidal because ρ̄|\_{G_F} is irreducible, their weights are parallel k(ρ̄), resp. 2, and above p they are unramified, resp. of conductor exponent at most 1, because F is unramified at p. These are witnesses for (α) and (β).

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- ρ̄ = E[p] for an elliptic curve E/ℚ of conductor prime to p, p ≥ 5, with E[p] irreducible: k(ρ̄) = 2, and the newform of E is at once the (α)- and the (β)-witness over ℚ.
- k(ρ̄) = p + 1 (très ramifiée): the (α)-witness has weight p + 1 and level prime to p, the (β)-witness has weight 2 and is Steinberg at p.
- For p = 2 and k(ρ̄) = 4 no (α) is asserted, as in KW II.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), §10.2, p. 92 — From 'ρ̄ modular' to (α) and (β); [27] is Gross, [12] Coleman–Voloch.; [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), Proof of Theorem 6.1, solvable-image paragraph, p. 54 — The classical form of (α) and (β) over ℚ..

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.5-component-patching"></a>

### Patching on a chosen local component

**Lemma.** `R22.5/component-patching`.

Let B = ⊗̂\_{v∈S} B_v, where each B_v is a quotient of the framed local lifting ring R^{□,ψ}\_v that is 𝒪-flat with B_v[1/p] geometrically integral and formally smooth over E. Then B is 𝒪-flat and B[1/p] is geometrically integral and formally smooth. Let R_B = R^{□,ψ}\_S ⊗̂\_{R^{□,loc,ψ}\_S} B, and suppose a Hecke eigensystem 𝔪 of level U (R22.1) whose framed module M is an R_B-module, with the Taylor–Wiles systems of R22.2. Then R_B → 𝕋^□ has p-power torsion kernel, M ⊗ E is finite projective and faithful over R_B[1/p], and every 𝒪′-point of R_B is modular. So a lift is modular as soon as, at every v ∈ S, it lies on the same irreducible component B_v of Spec R^{□,ψ}\_v[1/p] as a modular point.

**Hypotheses.**

- For KW II's rings B_v is the whole ring R̄^{□,ψ}\_v (R22.3). Kisin's refinement chooses B_v to be one irreducible component: the potentially Barsotti–Tate lifts of a fixed type, ordinary or not ordinary, whose geometry is requested from LocalGaloisDeformationRings R08.4 (and at p = 2 from R08.5).

**Prerequisites.** [R22.3/patched-ring-and-module](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.3-patched-ring-and-module), [R22.3/patched-support](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.3-patched-support), [R22.3/generic-fibre-r-equals-t](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.3-generic-fibre-r-equals-t), [R22.2/taylor-wiles-module-system](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.2-taylor-wiles-module-system), `LocalGaloisDeformationRings:R08.4`, `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.3/depth-auslander-buchsbaum-and-dimension-bounds`, `LocalGaloisDeformationRings:R08.4/rank-two-bt-components`, `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.4/completed-tensor-domains-and-components`, `DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra:R03.5/kisin-characteristic-zero-patching`.

**Proof route.**

- Import the completed-tensor theorem for complete Noetherian O-flat local algebras with geometrically integral, formally smooth generic fibres and the stated common coefficient/residue field. Use the fixed quotient components and coefficient enlargements; integrality of individual generic fibres without geometric integrality is insufficient.
- Kisin Proposition (3.3.1) with this B: patching (R22.3/patched-ring-and-module, with R^{□,loc} replaced by B) gives B⟦x⟧ ↠ R_∞ and M_∞ finite free over 𝒪⟦y⟧ of equal dimension; DeformationAndDerivedPatchingAlgebra R03.5/kisin-characteristic-zero-patching (Kisin Proposition (3.3.1) with Lemma (3.3.4), Auslander–Buchsbaum through R03.3) makes M_∞ ⊗ E projective and faithful over B⟦x⟧[1/p], and B⟦x⟧ ≅ R_∞.
- Reduce modulo (y_1, …, y_h) to obtain the faithfulness of M ⊗ E over R_B[1/p] (Kisin Theorem (3.4.11)); modular points are then all of Spec R_B[1/p].

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- B_v = R̄^{□,ψ}\_v for KW II's conditions recovers R22.3/generic-fibre-r-equals-t.
- If the modular point and ρ lie on different components (one ordinary, the other not), the lemma says nothing: this is why Kisin matches ordinarity.
- Geometric integrality matters: an integral but not geometrically integral B[1/p] can split into several components after enlarging E.

**Sources.** [KISIN-FFLAT-2009](https://people.math.harvard.edu/~kisin/dvifiles/bt.dvi), Proposition (3.3.1), p. 62; Theorem (3.4.11) and Lemma (3.4.12), pp. 69–70 — The patching criterion on a chosen domain B.; [KISIN-FFLAT-2009](https://people.math.harvard.edu/~kisin/dvifiles/bt.dvi), Lemma (3.4.12), p. 69 — Completed tensor products (the hat is lost in the text extraction)..

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.5-fontaine-laffaille-lifting"></a>

### Modularity lifting in the Fontaine–Laffaille range

**Theorem.** `R22.5/fontaine-laffaille-lifting`.

Let p > 3, F a totally real number field with p unramified in F, and ρ, ρ₀ : G_F → GL_2(𝒪) with ρ̄ = ρ̄₀, ρ₀ modular and ρ geometric. Suppose that for every σ : F ↪ L, HT_σ(ρ) = HT_σ(ρ₀) consists of two distinct integers differing by at most p − 2; that ρ and ρ₀ are crystalline at every v | p; and that ρ̄(G_F) ⊇ SL_2(𝔽\_p). Then ρ is modular.

**Hypotheses.**

- The residual hypothesis is Gee's big image, GlobalGaloisDeformations R04.5/image-hypotheses (2), which is different from KW II's cyclotomic irreducibility.
- The local rings are the Fontaine–Laffaille rings, formally smooth: R08.6/export-fontaine-laffaille-irreducible covers F_v = ℚ\_p with ρ̄\_v irreducible, and the general unramified case is requested from LocalGaloisDeformationRings R08.6.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.4/ihara-avoidance-comparison](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.4-ihara-avoidance-comparison), [R22.4/support-transfer-mod-lambda](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.4-support-transfer-mod-lambda), [R22.4/modularity-from-full-support](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.4-modularity-from-full-support), [R22.4/integral-r-equals-t-when-smooth](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.4-integral-r-equals-t-when-smooth), [R22.5/solvable-base-change-reduction](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.5-solvable-base-change-reduction), `GlobalGaloisDeformations:R04.5/image-hypotheses`, `LocalGaloisDeformationRings:R08.6/export-fontaine-laffaille-irreducible`, `LocalGaloisDeformationRings:R08.6`.

**Proof route.**

- Base change as in Gee §5.5, preserving the residual image and remaining unramified above p, to arrange even [F:ℚ], residual unramifiedness away from p, unipotent inertia for ρ and ρ₀ away from p, equal determinants, and ρ̄|G_{F_v} trivial with #k(v)≡1 mod p at the ramified auxiliary places v∈T_r. These are not the Taylor–Wiles places Q: Q is disjoint from T and ρ̄(Frob_v) has distinct eigenvalues there (Gee §5.6, p. 33).
- Patch with Ihara avoidance: the pair of problems of R22.4/ihara-avoidance-comparison, full support in the unipotent problem transferred from the irreducible 𝒮′ problem (R22.4/support-transfer-mod-lambda).
- R22.4/modularity-from-full-support.

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- The p-adic Tate module of an elliptic curve over F with good reduction above p ≥ 5, p unramified in F and ρ̄(G_F) ⊇ SL_2(𝔽\_p), once some crystalline weight-two modular ρ₀ lifts ρ̄: the Hodge–Tate weights {0, 1} differ by 1 ≤ p − 2.
- Weights differing by p − 1 are outside the range: Fontaine–Laffaille theory stops at p − 2.
- p = 3 is excluded even when the image is GL_2(𝔽\_3): the theorem needs p > 3.

**Sources.** [GEE-MLT-2022](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2202.05818v2), Theorem 5.2, p. 29 — Theorem 5.2, with the crystalline and big-image conditions (1)–(3) that follow..

**Planet.** Fontaine–Laffaille modularity lifting.

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.5-ordinary-overlap"></a>

### The exact overlap with ordinary modularity lifting

**Comparison.** `R22.5/ordinary-overlap`.

Type (C) lifts and crystalline endpoint lifts with residual weight p+1 are ordinary, and the prescribed modular witness is ordinary with the matching quotient character. On the subfamily satisfying every hypothesis of Ordinary R21.5/nearly-ordinary-irreducible-lifting—odd p, determinant finite-order times a positive cyclotomic power, locally distinguished residual characters with the specified quotient, a compatible ordinary modular witness, and the supplier’s exclusion of induction from a quadratic field with a complex place—the ordinary theorem and KW lifting give the same modularity conclusion. The local ordinary shape alone does not imply these additional hypotheses. The remaining ordinary cases continue to use KW patching or the requested Diamond endpoint route.

**Hypotheses.**

- Preserve the stable ordinary line and its quotient character under base change; split distinct unramified residual characters can label different ordinary components.
- Check residual distinguishedness and the non-CM-induction restriction individually. Cyclotomic absolute irreducibility alone does not imply the latter.
- The finite-order determinant factor and ordinary witness must match the normalization of the chosen Ordinary supplier.

**Prerequisites.** [R22.5/kw-odd-prime-lifting](#gl2modularitylifting-r22.5-kw-odd-prime-lifting), `LocalGaloisDeformationRings:R08.6/export-endpoint-weight`, `LocalGaloisDeformationRings:R08.6/export-semistable-weight-two-at-p`, `AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations:R19.6`, `OrdinaryAutomorphicFormsAndModularityLifting:R21.5/nearly-ordinary-irreducible-lifting`.

**Proof route.**

- Type (C) is by definition (γ\_vχ\_p ∗; 0 γ\_v): ordinary with sub-character γ\_vχ\_p.
- Weight p + 1 crystalline lifts with k(ρ̄) = p + 1 are ordinary (R08.6/export-endpoint-weight), and Theorem 8.4's π fitting these data is ordinary at v | p.
- A π Steinberg at v | p has ρ\_π|D_v of type (C) by local–global compatibility at p (requested from AutomorphicGaloisRepresentations R19.6), hence ordinary.
- Apply the ordinary supplier only after the distinguishedness, labelled quotient and non-CM-induction conditions are verified. The current full-scope ordinary target remains an unresolved extension, not a theorem used without its restriction.

**Check.** With the displayed hypotheses and the actual prerequisite objects, verify that the conclusion has exactly the stated ring, module, representation and action types.

**Acceptance checks.**

- 11a1 at p = 11 (split multiplicative reduction, a Tate curve): its 11-adic representation is (γχ\_11 ∗; 0 γ) at 11 with γ unramified, of type (C), inside the overlap.
- A non-ordinary crystalline weight-2 lift (a_p divisible by p) is of type (A) but outside the overlap.
- A crystalline lift of weight p + 1 with k(ρ̄) = 2 is not treated by KW II's argument (their §10.2 remark) and is not in the overlap either.

**Sources.** [KW2-2009](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~shekhar/papers/proofs.pdf), §10.2, p. 92 — The ordinary endpoint case, which KW II cite rather than reprove..

<a id="gl2modularitylifting-r22.5-strong-residual-modularity"></a>

### Strong residual modularity

**Definition.** `R22.5/strong-residual-modularity`.

For p>2, F totally real and ρ:G_{F,S}→GL₂(E), StronglyResiduallyModular(ρ) means: ρ is potentially Barsotti–Tate at every v|p, its determinant is cyclotomic times a finite-order character, and there exists a parallel-weight-two Hilbert eigenform f with ρ̄\_f≅ρ̄, not special at any v|p, and potentially ordinary at v exactly when ρ is.

**Hypotheses.**

- The potentially Barsotti–Tate and potentially ordinary predicates, and determinant convention, are those of Kisin (3.5.4).
